% QUESTION 1
% Number: Question 1
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A complex load (in [latex]\Omega[/latex]) is represented as [latex]\Gamma_L = 0.5\angle30^\circ[/latex] on the Smith chart. A co-axial cable with a characteristic impedance of [latex]50 \, \Omega[/latex] is connected to the load. The new input impedance of the load now moves to a diametrically opposite point on the same [latex]\Gamma[/latex] circle on the Smith chart. <br/><br/>Which option is the nearest input impedance of the cable connected load (in [latex]\Omega[/latex])?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]20.7 - j5.1[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]17.7 - j11.8[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]97.5 - j65.0[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]97.5 + j65.0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
Reflection coefficient of load: [latex]
\Gamma_L=0.5\angle30^\circ
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Characteristic impedance: [latex]
Z_0=50\Omega
[/latex]<br/><br/>
The input impedance moves to the <b>diametrically opposite point</b> on the same Smith chart circle.<br/><br/>
On a Smith chart, diametrically opposite point means:<br/>
[latex]
\Gamma_{in}=0.5\angle(30^\circ+180^\circ)
=
0.5\angle210^\circ
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Convert reflection coefficient into rectangular form</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\Gamma_{in}
=
0.5(\cos210^\circ+j\sin210^\circ)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
[latex]
=
0.5(-0.866-j0.5)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
[latex]
\Gamma_{in}
=
-0.433-j0.25
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Use impedance-reflection coefficient relation</b><br/><br/>
Substituting values,<br/>
[latex]
Z_{in}
=
50\cdot
\frac{1+(-0.433-j0.25)}
{1-(-0.433-j0.25)}
[/latex]
[latex]
=
50\cdot
\frac{0.567-j0.25}
{1.433+j0.25}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Multiplying numerator and denominator by conjugate:<br/>
[latex]
Z_{in}
=
50\cdot
\frac{(0.567-j0.25)(1.433-j0.25)}
{(1.433)^2+(0.25)^2}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
After simplification, [latex]
Z_{in}
\approx
17.7-j11.8\Omega
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 2
% Number: Question 2
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The cutoff frequency (in GHz) for the dominant [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode of an air-filled rectangular waveguide of inner dimension [latex]0.28 \text{ inch} \times 0.14 \text{ inch}[/latex] is ______. (rounded off to two decimal places)</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
Inner dimensions: [latex]a = 0.28 \text{ inch},\quad b = 0.14 \text{ inch}[/latex]<br/>
Air-filled waveguide, dominant mode: [latex]TE_{10}[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Convert dimensions to meters</b><br/><br/>
1 inch = 0.0254 m<br/>
[latex]
a = 0.28 \times 0.0254 = 7.112 \times 10^{-3} \text{ m}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Apply cutoff frequency formula for [latex]TE_{10}[/latex]</b><br/><br/>
For [latex]TE_{mn}[/latex] mode, cutoff frequency is:<br/>
[latex]
f_c = \frac{c}{2}\sqrt{\left(\frac{m}{a}\right)^2 + \left(\frac{n}{b}\right)^2}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
For [latex]TE_{10}[/latex]: [latex]m=1, n=0[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
f_c = \frac{c}{2a} = \frac{3 \times 10^8}{2 \times 7.112 \times 10^{-3}}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Calculate</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
f_c = \frac{3 \times 10^8}{1.4224 \times 10^{-2}} = 21.09 \times 10^9 \text{ Hz}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 3
% Number: Question 3
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Which option(s) represents/represent the dielectric loss tangent of a substrate?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Ratio of the real to imaginary parts of the total displacement current</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex](\omega\epsilon'' + \sigma)/(\omega\epsilon')[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Ratio of the electric susceptibility to permittivity</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Ratio of the polarization vector [latex]\vec{P}[/latex] to the displacement vector [latex]\vec{D}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>The loss tangent</b> [latex]\tan\delta[/latex] is defined as the ratio of the total loss current (conduction + displacement loss) to the lossless displacement current:<br/>
[latex]
\tan\delta = \frac{\omega\epsilon'' + \sigma}{\omega\epsilon'}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
(A) "Ratio of real to imaginary parts of displacement current", this is exactly [latex]\tan\delta[/latex]<br/>
(B) [latex]\frac{\omega\epsilon'' + \sigma}{\omega\epsilon'}[/latex], this is the standard formula<br/>
(C) "Ratio of electric susceptibility to permittivity", [latex]\chi/\epsilon[/latex], this is NOT the loss tangent<br/>
(D) "Ratio of [latex]\vec{P}[/latex] to [latex]\vec{D}[/latex]" , [latex]P/D = (\epsilon-\epsilon_0)/\epsilon[/latex], this is NOT the loss tangent<br/><br/>
<b>Answer:</b><br/>
Options (A) and (B) correctly represent the dielectric loss tangent.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 4
% Number: Question 4
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The electric field of a monochromatic plane wave travelling in a lossless isotropic and homogenous medium is given by<br/><br/> [latex]\vec{E}(z,t) = E_0[\hat{x}\cos(\omega t - kz) + \hat{y}\sin(\omega t - kz)][/latex]<br/><br/> in a right-handed orthogonal co-ordinate system. <br/><br/>Which of the following is the correct polarization of the electromagnetic wave?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Right-handed circularly polarized</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Left-handed circularly polarized</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linearly polarized</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linearly polarized with [latex]-45^\circ[/latex] angle to [latex]\hat{x}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given, <br/><br/>
[latex]
\vec{E}(z,t)=E_0\left[\cos(\omega t-kz)\hat{a}_x+\sin(\omega t-kz)\hat{a}_y\right]
[/latex]
<br/>
At [latex] z=0 [/latex], <br/><br/>
[latex]
\vec{E}(t)=E_0\cos\omega t , \hat{a}_x + E_0\sin\omega t , \hat{a}_y
[/latex]
<br/>
Here, <br/><br/>
[latex]
|E_{x0}|=|E_{y0}|=E_0
[/latex]
<br/>
and the phase difference is, <br/><br/>
[latex]
|\phi|=90^\circ
[/latex]
<br/>
Therefore, the wave is <b>circularly polarized</b>. <br/><br/>
Now, at [latex] t=0 [/latex], [latex] \omega t=0 [/latex], <br/><br/>
[latex]
\vec{E}=E_0\hat{a}_x
[/latex]
<br/>
At [latex] t=\dfrac{T}{4} [/latex], [latex] \omega t=\dfrac{\pi}{2} [/latex], <br/><br/>
[latex]
\vec{E}=E_0\hat{a}_y
[/latex]
<br/>
Thus, the electric field rotates from [latex] \hat{a}_x [/latex] toward [latex] \hat{a}_y [/latex] with time. <br/><br/>
Hence, it is a <b>Right-handed circularly polarized</b> wave.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 5
% Number: Question 5
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the Friis' transmission equation [latex]P_R = \frac{P_T G_T G_R \lambda^2}{(4\pi D)^2}[/latex], where [latex]P_R[/latex] and [latex]P_T[/latex] are the received and the transmitted powers, respectively. <br/><br/>[latex]G_T[/latex] and [latex]G_R[/latex] are the gain of transmitting and receiving antennas, respectively, [latex]D[/latex] is the distance between the transmitting and receiving antennas, and [latex]\lambda[/latex] is the wavelength in free space.<br/><br/>
Given: [latex]G_T = G_R = 1.0[/latex], [latex]\lambda = 0.30 \text{ m}[/latex] and [latex]P_T = +10 \text{ dBm}[/latex].<br/><br/> Choose the distance ([latex]D[/latex]), in km, from the following options at which the received power, [latex]P_R = -90 \text{ dBm}[/latex]?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{15}{4\pi}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{15}{2\pi}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{75}{2\pi}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3}{4\pi}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
[latex]G_T=G_R=1[/latex], [latex]\lambda=0.30\text{ m}[/latex], [latex]P_T=+10\text{ dBm}[/latex], [latex]P_R=-90\text{ dBm}[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Power ratio</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\frac{P_R}{P_T} = \frac{-90-10}{10}\text{ dB} = -100\text{ dB} = 10^{-10}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Friis equation</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\frac{P_R}{P_T} = G_T G_R \left(\frac{\lambda}{4\pi D}\right)^2 = 1\times1\times\left(\frac{0.3}{4\pi D}\right)^2
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Solve for D</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
10^{-10} = \left(\frac{0.3}{4\pi D}\right)^2
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
10^{-5} = \frac{0.3}{4\pi D}
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
D = \frac{0.3}{4\pi \times 10^{-5}} = \frac{0.3}{4\pi}\times10^5 = \frac{30000}{4\pi}\text{ m} = \frac{30}{4\pi}\text{ km} = \frac{15}{2\pi}\text{ km}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 6
% Number: Question 6
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Two resistors are connected in a circuit loop of area [latex]5 \mathrm{~m}^{2}[/latex], as shown in the figure below. The circuit loop is placed on the [latex]x-y[/latex] plane.<br/><br/>
When a time-varying magnetic flux, with fluxdensity [latex]\mathrm{B}(\mathrm{t})=0.5 \mathrm{t}[/latex] (in Tesla), is applied along the positive z-axis, the magnitude of current I (in Amperes, rounded off to two decimal places) in the loop is __________ . <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q55.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q55.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given: <br/>
Loop area, [latex] A = 5\ \text{m}^2 [/latex] <br/>
Magnetic field, [latex] B(t) = 0.5t\ \text{T} [/latex] <br/>
Total resistance in the loop = [latex]2\ \Omega + 2\ \Omega = 4\ \Omega[/latex] <br/>
<br/>
We use Faraday's Law of electromagnetic induction:
[latex] \mathcal{E} = -\frac{d\Phi}{dt} [/latex]
where [latex] \Phi = B(t) \cdot A [/latex] is the magnetic flux.
<br/>
So,
[latex] \Phi(t) = B(t) \cdot A = 0.5t \cdot 5 = 2.5t [/latex] <br/>
[latex] \frac{d\Phi}{dt} = \frac{d}{dt}(2.5t) = 2.5\ \text{V} [/latex] <br/>
<br/>
So, the induced EMF is:
[latex] \mathcal{E} = -2.5\ \text{V} [/latex]  <br/>
(The negative sign indicates direction, but we are interested in magnitude of current)
<br/> <br/>
Now, using Ohm's Law:
[latex] I = \frac{|\mathcal{E}|}{R} = \frac{2.5}{4} = 0.625\ \text{A} [/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 7
% Number: Question 7
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A [latex]50 \Omega[/latex] lossless transmission line is terminated with a load [latex]\mathrm{Z}_{\mathrm{L}}[/latex] of [latex](50-\mathrm{j} 75) \Omega[/latex].<br/><br/>
If the average incident power on the line is 10 mW , then the average power delivered to the load (in mW , rounded off to one decimal place) is __________ .</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Step 1: Characteristic Impedance
Given:
[latex] Z_0 = 50\ \Omega [/latex] (lossless line)<br/>
[latex] Z_L = 50 - j75\ \Omega [/latex] <br/>
Incident power = [latex] P_{\text{inc}} = 10\ \text{mW} [/latex] <br/><br/>Step 2: Reflection Coefficient
Reflection coefficient [latex] \Gamma = \frac{Z_L - Z_0}{Z_L + Z_0} [/latex] <br/>First compute:
[latex] Z_L + Z_0 = (50 - j75) + 50 = 100 - j75 [/latex] <br/>
[latex] Z_L - Z_0 = (50 - j75) - 50 = -j75 [/latex] <br/><br/>So:
[latex] \Gamma = \frac{-j75}{100 - j75} [/latex] <br/>Multiply numerator and denominator by the complex conjugate of the denominator:
[latex] \Gamma = \frac{-j75 \cdot (100 + j75)}{(100 - j75)(100 + j75)} [/latex] <br/>Numerator:
[latex] -j75(100 + j75) = -j7500 - j^2 75 \cdot 75 = -j7500 + 5625 [/latex] <br/>Denominator:
[latex] 100^2 + 75^2 = 10000 + 5625 = 15625 [/latex] <br/>So:
[latex] \Gamma = \frac{5625 - j7500}{15625} = 0.36 - j0.48 [/latex] <br/><br/>Step 3: Reflected Power
[latex] |\Gamma|^2 = (0.36)^2 + (0.48)^2 = 0.1296 + 0.2304 = 0.36 [/latex] <br/>
So, reflected power = [latex] P_{\text{ref}} = |\Gamma|^2 \cdot P_{\text{inc}} = 0.36 \cdot 10 = 3.6\ \text{mW} [/latex] <br/><br/>Step 4: Delivered Power
[latex] P_{\text{delivered}} = P_{\text{inc}} - P_{\text{ref}} = 10 - 3.6 = \boxed{6.4\ \text{mW}} [/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 8
% Number: Question 8
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An electric field of [latex]0.01 \mathrm{~V} / \mathrm{m}[/latex] is applied along the length of a copper wire of circular cross-section with diameter 1 mm . Copper has a conductivity of [latex]5.8 \times 10^{7} \mathrm{~S} / \mathrm{m}[/latex].<br/><br/>
The current (in Amperes, rounded off to two decimal places) flowing through the wire is __________ .</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.46</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.82</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.58</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.12</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\mathrm{J}=\sigma \mathrm{E}=0.01 \times 5.8 \times 10^{7}[/latex]<br/>
Current,
<br/>[latex]
\begin{aligned}
I &amp;=J \pi r^{2} \\
&amp; =0.01 \times 5.8 \times 10^{7} \times \pi \times\left(0.5 \times 10^{-3}\right)^{2} \\
&amp; =0.045 \times 10^{7} \times 10^{-6}=0.46 \mathrm{~A}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 9
% Number: Question 9
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A square metal sheet of [latex]4 \mathrm{~m} \times 4 \mathrm{~m}[/latex] is placed on the [latex]x-y[/latex] plane as shown in the figure below.<br/><br/>
If the surface charge density (in [latex]\mu \mathrm{C} / \mathrm{m}^{2}[/latex] ) on the sheet is [latex]\rho_{\mathrm{s}}(\mathrm{x}, \mathrm{y})=4|\mathrm{y}|[/latex], then the total charge (in [latex]\mu \mathrm{C}[/latex], rounded off to the nearest integer) on the sheet is<br/>
__________ . <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q39.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q39.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">16</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">85</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">64</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">256</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{Q} &amp;=\iint 4|\mathrm{y}| \mathrm{dx} \mathrm{dy}=4 \iint 4 \mathrm{ydx} \mathrm{dy} \\
&amp; =16 \int \mathrm{dx} \int \mathrm{ydy}=16[\mathrm{x}]_{0}^{2}\left[\frac{\mathrm{y}^{2}}{2}\right]_{0}^{2} \\
&amp; =16 \times 2 \times \frac{2^{2}}{2}=16 \times 4=64 \mu \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 10
% Number: Question 10
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A lossless transmission line with characteristic impedance [latex]Z_{0}=50 \Omega[/latex] is terminated with an unknown load. The magnitude of the reflection co-efficient is [latex]|\Gamma|=0.6[/latex]. As one moves towards the generator from the load, the maximum value of the input impedance magnitude looking towards the load (in [latex]\Omega[/latex] ) is ______</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
As we know that
<br/> [latex]
Z_{\max }=S Z_{0}
[/latex]<br/>
Now,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
S &amp; =\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}=\frac{1+0.6}{1-0.6}=\frac{1.6}{0.4}=4 \\
\therefore \quad Z_{\text {max }} &amp; =4 \times 50=200 \Omega .
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 11
% Number: Question 11
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A uniform plane wave with electric field [latex]\vec{E}(x)=A_{y} \hat{a}_{y} e^{-j \frac{2 \pi x}{3}} \mathrm{~V} / \mathrm{m}[/latex] is travelling in the air (relative permittivity, [latex]\epsilon_{r}=1[/latex] and relative permeability, [latex]\mu_{r}=1[/latex] ) in the [latex]+x[/latex] direction [latex]\left(A_{y}\right.[/latex] is a positive constant, [latex]\hat{a}_{y}[/latex] is the unit vector along the [latex]y[/latex] axis). It is incident normally on an ideal electric conductor (conductivity, [latex]\sigma=\infty[/latex] ) at [latex]x=0[/latex]. The position of the first null of the total magnetic field in the air (measured from [latex]x=0[/latex], in metres) is ________</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]-\frac{3}{4}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-\frac{3}{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-6[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-3[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a29.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a29.webp"/></noscript><br/>At perfect conductor,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{array}{ll}
&amp; \Gamma=-1 \\
\Rightarrow &amp; \Gamma=1 \mid \pi
\end{array}
[/latex]<br/>
As we know that [latex]H_{\min }[/latex] occurs at [latex]E_{\max }[/latex].Hence,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp; E_{\max }=E_{0}[1+|\Gamma|] \\
&amp; H_{\min }=\frac{E_{0}}{\eta}[1-|\Gamma|] \text { at } 2 \beta x_{\max }=2 n \pi+\theta_{\Gamma}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
So,
<br/> [latex]
2 \beta x_{\max }=2 n \pi+\theta_{\Gamma}
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \frac{4 \pi}{\lambda} x_{\max }=2 n \pi+\pi[/latex]
<br/> [latex]
\Rightarrow \quad x_{\max }=(2 n+1) \frac{\lambda}{4} ; n=0,1,2,3 \ldots
[/latex]<br/>
So, for [latex]1^{\text {st }}[/latex] value of [latex]x[/latex] for [latex]\mathrm{H}[/latex]-field to be zero is
<br/> [latex]
x_{\max }=\frac{\lambda}{4} \quad[\text { at } n=0]
[/latex]<br/>
Now, from the equation given,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{array}{rlrl}
\beta &amp; =\frac{2 \pi}{3} \\
\Rightarrow &amp; \frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} &amp; =\frac{2 \pi}{3} \\
\Rightarrow \quad &amp; \lambda &amp; =3
\end{array}
[/latex]<br/>
Hence,
<br/> [latex]
x_{\max }=\frac{3}{4}
[/latex]<br/>
If the reference is not at interference then [latex]x=-\frac{3}{4}[/latex].</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 12
% Number: Question 12
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Let [latex]\hat{i}[/latex] and [latex]\hat{j}[/latex] be the unit vectors along [latex]x[/latex] and [latex]y[/latex] axes, respectively and let [latex]A[/latex] be a positive constant. Which one of the following statements is true for the vector fields [latex]\vec{F}_{1}=A(\hat{i} y+\hat{j} x)[/latex] and [latex]\vec{F}_{2}=A(\hat{i} y-\hat{j} x) ?[/latex]</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Both [latex]\vec{F}_{1}[/latex] and [latex]\vec{F}_{2}[/latex] are electrostatic fields.</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Only [latex]\vec{F}_{1}[/latex] is an electrostatic field.</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Only [latex]\vec{F}_{2}[/latex] is an electrostatic field.</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Neither [latex]\vec{F}_{1}[/latex] nor [latex]\vec{F}_{2}[/latex] is an electrostatic field.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For an electrostatic field, [latex]\nabla \times \bar{F}=0[/latex]
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp; \bar{F}_{1}=A[y \hat{i}+x \hat{j}] \\
&amp; \Rightarrow \\
&amp; \Delta \times \bar{F}_{1}=A\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{i} &amp; \hat{j} &amp; \hat{k} \\
\partial / \partial x &amp; \partial / \partial y &amp; \partial / \partial z \\
y &amp; x &amp; 0
\end{array}\right| \\
&amp; =A[0 \hat{i}-0 \hat{j}+(1-1) \hat{k}] \\
&amp; =0 \\
&amp; \bar{F}_{2}=A[y \hat{i}-x \hat{j}] \\
&amp; \Rightarrow \\
&amp; \Delta \times \bar{F}_{2}=A\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{i} &amp; \hat{j} &amp; \hat{k} \\
\partial / \partial x &amp; \partial / \partial y &amp; \partial / \partial z \\
y &amp; -x &amp; 0
\end{array}\right| \\
&amp; =A[0 \hat{i}-0 \hat{j}+(-1-1) \hat{k}] \\
&amp; =-2 A \hat{k}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
Hence, [latex]\bar{F}_{1}[/latex] is electrostatic, [latex]\bar{F}_{2}[/latex] is not electrostatic.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 13
% Number: Question 13
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a lossless transmission line terminated with a short circuit as shown in the figure below. As one moves towards the generator from the load, the normalized impedances [latex]z_{\text {in } A}, z_{\text {in } B}, z_{\text {in } C}[/latex] and [latex]z_{\text {in } D}[/latex] (indicated in the figure) are _____ <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q5.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q5.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]z_{\text {in } A}=+0.4 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } B}=\infty, z_{\text {in } C}=-0.4 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } D}=0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]z_{\text {in } A}=\infty, z_{\text {in } B}=+0.4 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } C}=0, z_{\text {in } D}=+0.4 j \Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]z_{\text {in } A}=-1 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } B}=0, z_{\text {in } C}=+1 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } D}=\infty[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]z_{\text {in } A}=+1 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } B}=\infty, z_{\text {in } C}=-1 j \Omega, z_{\text {in } D}=0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a5.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a5.webp"/></noscript><br/>[latex]Z_{s / c}=j Z_{0} \tan \beta l[/latex].
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] Normalized impedance,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp; \bar{Z}_{S / C}=\frac{Z_{S / C}}{Z_{0}}=j \tan \beta l \\
&amp; \bar{Z}_{\text {in } A}=j \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{\lambda}{8}\right)=j \tan (\pi / 4)=j 1 \Omega \\
&amp; \bar{Z}_{\text {in } B}=j \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{\lambda}{4}\right)=\infty \\
&amp; \bar{Z}_{\text {in } C}=j \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{3 \lambda}{8}\right)=-j 1 \Omega \\
&amp; \bar{Z}_{\text {in } D}=j \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{\lambda}{2}\right)=0
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 14
% Number: Question 14
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A transparent dielectric coating is applied to glass [latex]\left(\epsilon_{r}=4, \mu_{r}=1\right)[/latex] to eliminate the reflection of red light [latex]\left(\lambda_{0}=0.75 \mu \mathrm{m}\right)[/latex]. The minimum thickness of the dielectric coating, in [latex]\mu \mathrm{m}[/latex], that can be used is ____ (rounded off to two decimal places).</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For no reflection, impedance must be matched.
<br/>
Hence, [latex]\eta_{2}[/latex] acts like a quarter wave impedance transformer.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a50.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a50.jpg"/></noscript><br/>So,
<br/>
[latex]
(i) \quad \eta_{2}=\sqrt{\eta_{1} \cdot \eta_{3}} \Rightarrow \epsilon_{r_{2}}=\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{1}} \cdot \epsilon_{r_{3}}} \Rightarrow \epsilon_{r_{2}}=2
[/latex]
<br/><br/>
(ii) For impedance matching,
<br/>
[latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp; d=(2 n+1) \frac{\lambda}{4} ; n=0,1,2 \ldots \\
&amp; \lambda=\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}}=\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{2}}}} \\
Here \;\;&amp; \lambda=\frac{0.75 \times 10^{-6}}{\sqrt{2}}=0.53 \times 10^{-6}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]
<br/>
Hence, for minimum distance, [latex]n=0[/latex]
<br/>
So,[latex]
d=\frac{\lambda}{4}=\frac{0.53 \times 10^{-6}}{4}=0.133 \mu \mathrm{m}
[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 16
% Number: Question 16
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The electric field of a plane electromagnetic wave is <br/><br/>[latex]E=a_{x} C_{1 x} \cos (\omega t-\beta z)+a_{y} C_{1 y} \cos (\omega t-\beta z+\theta) \mathrm{V} / \mathrm{m}[/latex] <br/><br/> Which of the following combination(s) will give rise to a left handed elliptically polarized (LHEP) wave?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]C_{1 x}=1, C_{1 y}=1, \theta=\pi / 4[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]C_{1 x}=2, C_{1 y}=1, \theta=\pi / 2[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]C_{1 x}=1, C_{1 y}=2, \theta=3 \pi / 2[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]C_{1 x}=2, C_{1 y}=1, \theta=3 \pi / 4[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given, [latex]\quad \vec{E}=\hat{a}_{x} C_{1 x} \cos (\omega t-\beta z)+\hat{a}_{y} C_{1 y} \cos (\omega t-\beta z+\theta)[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]z=0[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]
\vec{E}=C_{1 x} \cos \omega t \hat{a}_{x}+C_{1 y} \cos (\omega t+\theta) \hat{a}_{y}
[/latex]
<br/>
Going by options,
<br/><br/>
Option (A) [latex]\quad \vec{E}=\cos \omega t \hat{a}_{x}+\cos (\omega t+\pi / 4) \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=0, \omega t=0, \vec{E}=\hat{a}_{x}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=T / 4, \omega t=\pi / 2, \vec{E}=0-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] Hence, it is LHEP.
<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43a.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43a.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
<br/>Option (B) [latex]\quad \vec{E}=2 \cos \omega t \hat{a}_{x}+\cos (\omega t+\pi / 2) \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=0, \omega t=0, \vec{E}=2 \hat{a}_{x}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=T / 4, \omega t=\pi / 2, \vec{E}=-1 \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] Hence, it is LHEP.
<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43b.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43b.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>
Option (C) [latex]\quad \vec{E}=\cos \omega t \hat{a}_{x}+2 \cos (\omega t+3 \pi / 2) \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=0, \quad \quad \omega t=0, \vec{E}=\hat{a}_{x}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=T / 4, \omega t=\pi / 2, \vec{E}=2 \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] Hence, it is RHEP.
<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43c.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43c.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>
Option (D) [latex]\quad \vec{E}=2 \cos \omega t \hat{a}_{x}+\cos (\omega t+3 \pi / 4) \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=0, \omega t=0, \vec{E}=2 \hat{a}_{x}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
at [latex]t=T / 4, \omega t=\pi / 2, \vec{E}=0-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{a}_{y}=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{2}} \hat{a}_{y}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] Hence, it is LHEP.
<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43d.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a43d.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
[latex]\therefore[/latex] Option (A), (B) and (D) are correct.</div>

% QUESTION 17
% Number: Question 17
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The standing wave ratio on a [latex]50 \Omega[/latex] lossless transmission line terminated in an unknown load impedance is found to be 2.0. The distance between successive voltage minima is [latex]30 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex] and the first minimum is located at [latex]10 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex] from the load. [latex]Z_{L}[/latex] can be replaced by an equivalent length [latex]l_{m}[/latex] and terminating resistance [latex]R_{m}[/latex] of the same line. The value of [latex]R_{m}[/latex] and [latex]l_{m}[/latex], respectively, are
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q42.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q42.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]R_{m}=100 \Omega, l_{m}=20 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]R_{m}=25 \Omega, l_{m}=20 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]R_{m}=100 \Omega, l_{m}=5 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]R_{m}=25 \Omega, l_{m}=5 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given [latex]S=2, Z_{\min }=10 \mathrm{~cm}, Z_{0}=50 \Omega[/latex]
<br/>
As we know that, [latex]\quad|\Gamma|=\frac{S-1}{S+1}=\frac{2-1}{2+1}=\frac{1}{3}[/latex]
<br/>
Now, distance between successive voltage minima [latex]=30 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \frac{\lambda}{2}=30 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \lambda=60 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]
<br/><br/>
Also, for minima,
<br/>
[latex]
2 \beta Z_{\min }=(2 n+1) \pi+\theta_{\Gamma}
[/latex]
<br/><br/>
At [latex]n=0,1 \mathrm{st}[/latex] minima, [latex]Z_{\text {min }}=10 \mathrm{~cm}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]
\frac{4 \pi}{\lambda} Z_{\min }=\pi+\theta_{\Gamma}
[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \frac{4 \pi}{60} * 10=\pi+\theta_{\Gamma}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \frac{2 \pi}{3}-\pi=\theta_{\Gamma}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad \theta_{\Gamma}=\frac{-\pi}{3} \quad \therefore \Gamma=\frac{1}{3} \angle-60^{\circ}[/latex]
<br/><br/>
Now,[latex]
\Gamma=\frac{Z_{L}-Z_{0}}{Z_{L}+Z_{0}}
[/latex]
<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a42.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a42.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
[latex]\begin{array}{ll}\Rightarrow \quad &amp; Z_{L}=Z_{0}\left[\frac{1+\Gamma}{1-\Gamma}\right] \\ \Rightarrow &amp; Z_{L}=50\left[\frac{1+0.33 e^{-j \frac{\pi}{3}}}{1-0.33 e^{-j \frac{\pi}{3}}}\right]\end{array}[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad Z_{L}=67.97 \angle-32.67^{\circ}[/latex]
<br/>Now [latex]Z_{\text {in }}=Z_{0}\left[\frac{Z_{L}+j Z_{0} \tan \beta l}{Z_{0}+j Z_{L} \tan \beta l}\right][/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \quad Z_{\text {in }}=50\left[\frac{R_{m}+j 50 \tan \beta l_{m}}{50+j R_{m} \tan \beta l_{m}}\right][/latex]
<br/>
Here, [latex]\quad Z_{\text {in }}=Z_{L}=67.97 \angle-32.67^{\circ}[/latex]
<br/>
Going through options,
<br/>
[latex]\left.\begin{array}{l}R_{m}=100 \Omega \text { and } L_{m}=5 \mathrm{~cm} \\ R_{m}=25 \Omega \text { and } L_{m}=20 \mathrm{~cm}\end{array}\right\}[/latex] satisfy this identity, hence option (B) and (C) are correct.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A cascade of common-source amplifiers in a unity gain feedback configuration oscillates when</div>

<br/> Loop gain magnitude [latex]\geq 1[/latex].

% QUESTION 19
% Number: Question 19
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a narrow band signal, propagating in a lossless dielectric medium [latex]\left(\epsilon_{r}=4, \mu_{r}=1\right)[/latex], with phase velocity [latex]v_{p}[/latex] and group velocity [latex]v_{g}[/latex]. Which of the following statement is true? ( [latex]c[/latex] is the velocity of light in vacuum.)</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]v_{p} \gt c, v_{g} \gt c[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]v_{p} \lt c, v_{g} \gt c[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]v_{p} \gt c, v_{g} \lt c[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]v_{p} \lt c, v_{g} \lt c[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Phase velocity, [latex]V_{p}=\frac{\omega}{\beta}=\frac{\omega}{\omega \sqrt{\mu \epsilon}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0} \epsilon_{0}}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{r} \epsilon_{r}}}=\frac{C}{\sqrt{\mu_{r} \epsilon_{r}}}[/latex]
<br/>[latex]\therefore \quad V_{p} \lt C[/latex]
<br/>
Group velocity, [latex]V_{g}=\frac{d \omega}{d \beta}=\frac{V_{p}}{1-\frac{\omega}{V_{p}} \frac{d V_{p}}{d \omega}}[/latex]
<br/>
Here, [latex]\quad V_{p} \neq f(\omega)[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\therefore \quad V_{g}=V_{p} \lt C[/latex]
<br/>
Hence, [latex]\quad V_{p} \lt C[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]\quad \quad V_{g} \lt C[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 20
% Number: Question 20
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2022, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">In an electrostatic field, the electric displacement density vector, [latex] \vec{D}  [/latex], is given by<br/>[latex] \vec{D}(x,y,z)=(x^3\vec{i}+y^3\vec{j}+xy^2\vec{k})C/m^2  [/latex]<br/>,
where [latex] \vec{i},\vec{j},\vec{k}  [/latex] are the unit vectors along x-axis, y-axis, and z-axis, respectively.
Consider a cubical region R centered at the origin with each side of length 1 m, and
vertices at ([latex]\pm 0.5 m, \pm 0.5 m, \pm 0.5 m  [/latex]). The electric charge enclosed within R is
_________ C (rounded off to two decimal places).</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{D}(x,y,z)=(x^3\vec{i}+y^3\vec{j}+xy^2\vec{k})c/m^2[/latex]<br/>
[latex]Q_{enc.}=\int _v \rho _v\cdot dV=\int (\triangledown \cdot \vec{D})dV[/latex]<br/>
[latex]\triangledown \cdot \vec{D}=3x^2+3y^2[/latex]<br/>
[latex]dV=dxdydz[/latex]<br/>
[latex]\therefore \; Q_{enc.}=\int _v 3(x^2+y^2)dxdydz =3\left [ \int_{-0.5}^{0.5} x^2 dx  \int_{-0.5}^{0.5}dy \int_{-0.5}^{0.5} dz+\int_{-0.5}^{0.5}dx\int_{-0.5}^{0.5}y^2dy\int_{-0.5}^{0.5}dz \right ][/latex]<br/>[latex] =3\left [ \frac{x^3}{3}|_{-0.5}^{0.5} \times 1 \times 1 + \frac{y^3}{3}|_{-0.5}^{0.5} \times 1 \times 1\right ] =0.25+0.25=0.5C[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 21
% Number: Question 21
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2022, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A waveguide consists of two infinite parallel plates (perfect conductors) at a
separation of [latex] 10^{-4}[/latex] cm, with air as the dielectric. Assume the speed of light in air to
be [latex]3 \times  10^{8}[/latex] m/s. The frequency/frequencies of TM waves which can propagate in
this waveguide is/are _______.</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]6 \times 10^{15}Hz[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.5 \times 10^{12}Hz[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]8 \times 10^{14}Hz[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1 \times 10^{13}Hz[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Cut-off frequency.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned}
f_c&amp;=\frac{c}{2a}\;\;\;(m=1)
&amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^8}{2 \times 10^{-4} \times 10^{-2}}\\
&amp;=1.5 \times 10^{14}Hz
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>
[latex]f \gt f_c [/latex] will only propagate<br/> A and C will propage.</div>

Which of the given options is/are solution(s) to the given wave equation?</div>

% QUESTION 24
% Number: Question 24
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An antenna with a directive gain of [latex]6\text{ dB}[/latex]
is radiating a total power of [latex]\text{16 kW}[/latex]. The amplitude of the electric field in free space at a distance of [latex]\text{8 km}[/latex]
from the antenna in the direction of [latex]\text{6 dB}[/latex]
gain (rounded off to three decimal places) is  ______  [latex]\text{V/m}[/latex]
.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G_{d}(\mathrm{~dB}) &amp;=10 \log _{10}\left(G_{d}\right) \\ \Rightarrow\qquad 6 &amp;=10 \log _{10} G_{d} \\ \Rightarrow\qquad G_{d} &amp;=10^{0.6} \\ G_{d} &amp;=3.981 \\ E_{m} &amp;=\frac{\sqrt{60 G_{d} P_{\text {rad }}}}{r} \\ E_{m} &amp;=\frac{\sqrt{60\left(16 \times 10^{3}\right)(3.981)}}{8 \times 10^{3}}=0.244(\mathrm{~V} / \mathrm{m}) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 25
% Number: Question 25
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A standard air-filled rectangular waveguide with dimensions [latex]\text{a=8 cm, b=4 cm}[/latex], operates at [latex]\text{3.4 GHz}[/latex]. For the dominant mode of wave propagation, the phase velocity of the signal in [latex]v_{p}[/latex]. The value (rounded off to two decimal places) of [latex]v_{p}/c[/latex], where c denotes the velocity of light, is _____</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]f_{c 10}=\frac{c}{2 a}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2\left(8 \times 10^{-2}\right)}=1.875 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex]<br/> Guide phase velocity,[latex] V_{p}=\frac{C}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{f_{C 10}}{f}\right)^{2}}} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{V_{p}}{C}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{f_{C 10}}{f}\right)^{2}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{1.875}{3.4}\right)^{2}}}=1.198[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 26
% Number: Question 26
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For a vector field [latex]D=\rho\cos^{2}\:\varphi \:a_{\rho }+z^{2}\sin^{2}\:\varphi \:a_{\varphi }[/latex]
in a cylindrical coordinate system [latex]\left ( \rho ,\varphi ,z \right )[/latex]
with unit vectors [latex]a_{\rho },a_{\varphi }[/latex]
and [latex]a_{z}[/latex]
, the net flux of D leaving the closed surface of the cylinder [latex]\left ( \rho =3, 0\leq z\leq 2 \right )[/latex]
(rounded off to two decimal places) is ________________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Method 1: [latex]\vec{D}=\rho \cos ^{2} \phi \hat{a}_{\rho}+z^{2} \sin ^{2} \phi \hat{a}_{\phi}[/latex]<br/> Electric flux crossing the closed surface is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \psi &amp;=\oiint \vec{D} \cdot \overrightarrow{d S}=\iiint(\vec{\nabla} \cdot \vec{D}) d v \\ \vec{\nabla} \cdot \vec{D} &amp;=\frac{1}{\rho} \frac{\partial}{\partial p}\left(\rho D_{\rho}\right)+\frac{1}{\rho} \frac{\partial D_{\phi}}{\partial \phi}+\frac{\partial D_{z}}{\partial z} \\ &amp;=\frac{1}{\rho} \frac{\partial}{\partial p}\left(\rho \rho \cos ^{2} \phi\right)+\frac{1}{\rho} \frac{\partial}{\partial \phi}\left(z^{2} \sin ^{2} \phi\right)+0 \\ &amp;=\frac{1}{\rho}(2 \rho) \cos ^{2} \phi+\frac{1}{\rho} z^{2} 2 \sin \phi \cos \phi=2 \cos ^{2} \phi+\frac{z^{2}}{\rho} \sin 2 \phi\\ \iiint(\vec{\nabla} \cdot \vec{D}) d v &amp;=\iiint 2 \cos ^{2} \phi(\rho d \rho d \phi d z)+\iiint\left(\frac{z^{2}}{\rho} \sin 2 \phi\right) \rho d \rho d \phi d z \\ &amp;=2 \int_{\rho=0}^{3} \rho d \rho \int_{\phi=0}^{2 \pi}\left(\frac{1+\cos 2 \phi}{2}\right) d \phi \int_{z=0}^{2} d z+\int_{\rho=0}^{2} d \rho \int_{\phi=0}^{2 \pi} \sin 2 \phi d \phi \int_{z=0}^{2} z^{2} d z \\ &amp;=2\left(\frac{\rho^{2}}{-2}\right)_{\rho=0}^{3} \frac{1}{2}(2 \pi)(z)_{z=0}^{2}+0 \\ &amp;=2\left(\frac{3^{2}}{2}\right) \pi(2)=18 \pi(\text { Coulomb })=56.55(\text { Coulomb }) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Method 2: Electric flux crossing the closed surface is<br/>[latex] \psi=\oiint \vec{D} \cdot \overrightarrow{d S}[/latex]<br/> Electric flux crossing \rho=3 cylindrical surface is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \left.\psi\right|_{\rho=3} &amp;=\oiint\left(\rho \cos ^{2} \phi \hat{a}_{p}\right) \cdot(\rho d \phi d z) \hat{a}_{p} \\ &amp;=3^{2} \int_{\phi=0}^{2 \pi} \cos ^{2} \phi d \phi \int_{z=0}^{2} d z \\ &amp;=9 \frac{1}{2}(2 \pi)(2)=18 \pi(\text { coulomb })=56.55(\text { coulomb }) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

C) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> The input impedance of [latex]\frac{\lambda}{4}[/latex] transmission line, as well as [latex]\frac{\lambda}{2}[/latex] transmission line is 00 , and they are in parallel with main transmission line, so they are not effective for main.<br/> Transmission line.<br/> Final configuration of given line is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/a44.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/a44.jpg"/></noscript><br/> For impedance matching, [latex]Z_{L}=\frac{Z_{1}^{2}}{Z_{0}}=\frac{(75)^{2}}{50}=112.5[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The refractive indices of the core and cladding of an optical fiber are 1.50 and 1.48, respectively. % QUESTION 29
% Number: Question 29
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the vector field [latex]F\:=\:a_{x}\left ( 4y-c_{1}z \right )+a_y\left ( 4x + 2z\right )+a_{z}\left ( 2y +z\right )[/latex] in a rectangular coordinate system (x,y,z) with unit vectors [latex]a_{x},\:a_{y}[/latex] and [latex]a_{z}[/latex]. If the field F is irrotational (conservative), then the constant [latex]c_{1}[/latex]
(in integer) is _________________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \nabla \times \vec{F}&amp;=0\\ \left|\begin{array}{ccc} i &amp; j &amp; k \\ \frac{\partial}{\partial x} &amp; \frac{\partial}{\partial y} &amp; \frac{\partial}{\partial z} \\ 4 y-c_{1} z &amp; 4 x+2 z &amp; 2 y+z \end{array}\right|&amp;=0\\ =i(2-2)-j\left(0+c_{1}\right)+k(4-4)=0 \\ c_{1} &amp;=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 31
% Number: Question 31
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2020, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The magnetic field of a uniform plane wave in vacuum is given by <br/><br/>[latex]\vec{H}(x,y,z,t)=(\hat{a}_x+2\hat{a}_y+b\hat{a}_z) \cos (\omega t+3x-y-z)[/latex]<br/><br/> The value of b is _____</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For uniform plane wave<br/>[latex]\hat{a}_{H}\cdot \hat{a}_{\rho }=0[/latex]
<br/>[latex]\hat{a}_{H}[/latex]is unit vector in magnetic field direction [latex]\hat{a}_{\rho }[/latex] is unit vector in power flow direction<br/>[latex]\hat{a}_{H }=\frac{1\hat{a}_{x}+2\hat{a}_{y}+b\hat{a}_{z}}{\sqrt{1^{2}+2^{2}+b^{2}}} [/latex]<br/>[latex]\hat{a}_{\rho }=\frac{-3\hat{a}_{x}+\hat{a}_{y}+\hat{a}_{z}}{\sqrt{3^{2}+1^{2}+1^{2}}}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \hat{a}_{H}\cdot \hat{a}_{\rho }=0 (\hat{a}_{x}+2\hat{a}_{y}+b\hat{a}_{z})\cdot (-3\hat{a}_{x}+\hat{a}_{y}+\hat{a}_{z})=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] -3+2+b=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] b=1[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For an infinitesimally small dipole in free space, the electric field [latex]E_\theta[/latex] in the far field
is proportional to [latex](e^{-jkr}/r) \sin \theta[/latex], where [latex]k = 2 \pi /\lambda[/latex]. A vertical infinitesimally small
electric dipole ([latex]\delta l \lt \lt \lambda[/latex]) is placed at a distance [latex]h(h \gt 0)[/latex] above an infinite ideal
conducting plane, as shown in the figure. The minimum value of h, for which one
of the maxima in the far field radiation pattern occurs at [latex]\theta =60^{\circ}[/latex], is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/q43.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/q43.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\lambda[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.5[latex]\lambda[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.25[latex]\lambda[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.75[latex]\lambda[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/a41.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/a41.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned}\left | \text{Total E }\right |&amp;=\left | (E_{\text{single element}}) \right | \left | (A.F.) \right | \\ \left | (A.F.) \right |&amp;=\frac{\sin\left ( N\frac{\psi }{2} \right )}{\sin (\frac{\psi }{2})}=\frac{\sin \left ( 2\frac{\psi }{2} \right )}{\sin \left ( \frac{\psi }{2} \right )} \\ &amp; =\frac{2\sin \left ( \frac{\psi }{2} \right )\cos (\frac{\psi }{2})}{\sin \frac{\psi }{2}} \\ &amp; =2\cos (\frac{\psi }{2})\\  \left | A.F_{N} \right | &amp;=\frac{A.F}{A.F_{max}}=\frac{2\cos (\frac{\psi }{2})}{2} \\ &amp;=\left | \cos \left ( \frac{\psi }{2} \right ) \right | \\
\text{where, }\psi &amp;=\beta d\cos \theta =\frac{2\pi }{\lambda }(2h)\cos \theta  \\ \left.\begin{matrix} \left | A.F_{N} \right |_{\theta =60^{\circ}} \end{matrix}\right|&amp; =\left | \cos (\frac{2\pi }{\lambda }h\cos 60^{\circ}) \right |\\ &amp;=\left | \cos \left ( \frac{\pi h}{\lambda } \right ) \right |\\ \cos \theta  \text{ is maximum ,where   } \theta &amp;=n\pi \;\; n=0,1,2..\\ \frac{\pi h}{\lambda }&amp;=n\pi \Rightarrow h=n\lambda \\ \Rightarrow \; \text{For } n=1,  \;\; h_{min}&amp;=\lambda \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 35
% Number: Question 35
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A rectangular waveguide of width w and height h has cut-off frequencies for [latex]TE_{10} \; and \;TE_{11}[/latex] modes in the ratio 1:2. The aspect ratio w/h, rounded off to two decimal places, is ___________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]f_{c m n}=\frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{m}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{n}{b}\right)^{2}}[/latex]<br/> For TE mode<br/>[latex] f_{c10}=\frac{c}{2 w} \quad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> and For [latex]TE_{11}[/latex] mode.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{c11} &amp;=\frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\frac{1}{w}^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{2}} &amp;\ldots(i)\\ &amp;=\frac{c}{2 w} \sqrt{1+\left(\frac{w}{h}\right)^{2}} &amp;\ldots(ii)\\ \text{given}\quad\frac{f_{c10}}{f_{11}} &amp;=\frac{1}{2} &amp;\ldots(iii) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> put (i). (in) in (ii)<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \frac{\frac{c}{2 w}}{\frac{c}{2 w} \sqrt{1+\left(\frac{w}{h}\right)^{2}}}=\frac{1}{2} \Rightarrow \sqrt{1+\left(\frac{w}{h}\right)^{2}}=2[/latex]<br/> On solving above equation, we get,<br/>[latex] \frac{w}{h}=\sqrt{3}=1.732[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 36
% Number: Question 36
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The dispersion equation of a waveguide,which relates the wavenumber kto the frequency [latex]\omega[/latex], is <br/><br/>[latex]k(\omega) =(1/c)\sqrt{\omega ^2 -{\omega _0}^2}[/latex]
<br/><br/> where the speed of light [latex]c=3 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s, and [latex]\omega_0[/latex] is a constant.  If the group velocity is [latex]2 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s, then the phase velocity is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.5 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]4.5 \times 10^8[/latex] m/s</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
By definition [latex]v_{p}=\frac{\omega}{\beta}=\frac{\omega}{k} [/latex]<br/> where, [latex]k(\omega)=\left(\frac{1}{c}\right) \sqrt{\omega^{2}-\omega_{0}^{2}} \quad [/latex](given)<br/>[latex] \therefore v_{p}=\frac{c}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{\omega_{0}}{\omega}\right)^{2}}}[/latex]<br/> by definition,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} v_{g} &amp;=\frac{d \omega}{d \beta}=\frac{d \omega}{d k} \\ &amp;=\frac{d k}{d \omega}=\frac{1}{c} \frac{1}{2 \sqrt{\omega^{2}-\omega_{0}^{2}}} \times 2 \omega \\ \text{or}\quad v_{g} &amp;=c \sqrt{1-\left(\frac{\omega_{0}}{\omega}\right)^{2}}\\ \because \quad v_{p} \cdot v_{g} &amp;=c^{2} \\ \therefore \quad v_{p} &amp;=\frac{c^{2}}{v_{g}}=\frac{\left(3 \times 10^{8}\right)^{2}}{2 \times 10^{8}} \\ &amp;=4.5 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 37
% Number: Question 37
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Two identical copper wires W1 and W2, placed in parallel as shown in the figure, carry currents I and 2I, respectively, in opposite directions. If the two wires are separated by a distance of 4r, then the magnitude of the magnetic field [latex]\vec{B}[/latex] between the wires at a distance r from W1 is  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q36.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q36.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\mu _0 I}{6 \pi r}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{6 \mu _0 I}{5 \pi r}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5 \mu _0 I}{6 \pi r}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{{\mu _0}^2 I^2}{2 \pi r^2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/a36.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/a36.jpg"/></noscript><br/>Magnetic flux density [latex](\vec{B})[/latex] at r distance due to infinite line carrying current I is [latex]|\vec{B}|=\frac{\mu_{0} I}{2 \pi \rho} .[/latex]<br/>[latex] \vec{B} [/latex] at r distance due to [latex]W_{1}[/latex] wire<br/>[latex] =\left|\vec{B}_{1}\right|=\frac{\mu_{0} I}{2 \pi r}\qquad \ldots(i)[/latex]<br/>[latex] \vec{B} [/latex] at 3r distance due to [latex]W_{2}[/latex] wire<br/>[latex] =\left|\vec{B}_{2}\right|=\frac{\mu_{0}(2 I)}{2 \pi(3 r)}\qquad \ldots(ii)[/latex]<br/> From right hand thumb rule, [latex]\vec{B} [/latex] due to both lines add in between conductors.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} {So,}\qquad |\vec{B}|&amp;=\left|\vec{B}_{1}\right|+\left|\vec{B}_{2}\right|\\ \therefore \qquad |\vec{B}|&amp;=\frac{\mu_{0} I}{2 \pi r}+\frac{2 \mu_{0} I}{6 \pi r}=\frac{5 \mu_{0} I}{6 \pi r} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 38
% Number: Question 38
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Radiation resistance of a small dipole current element of length l at a frequency of 3 GHz is 3 ohms. If the length is changed by 1%, then the percentage change in the radiation resistance, rounded off to two decimal places, is _____ %.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Radiation resistance of a small dipole current  element of length 'I' is <br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} R_{\mathrm{rad}}=80 \pi^{2}\left(\frac{l}{\lambda}\right)^{2} \Rightarrow R \propto l^{2} \\ \frac{R_{2}}{R_{1}}=\left(\frac{l_{2}}{l_{1}}\right)^{2} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> If length is changed by 1% then percentage change in the radiation resistance.<br/>[latex] \frac{R_{2}}{R_{1}}=\left(\frac{1.01 l}{l}\right)^{2}=1.0201[/latex]<br/>[latex] \text { Percentage change in radiation resistance } [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} =\frac{R_{2}-R_{1}}{R_{1}} \times 100 \\ =0.0201 \times 100=2.01 \% \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% Number: Question 39
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In the table shown, List I and List II, respectively, contain terms appearing on the left-hand side and the right-hand side of Maxwell's equations (in their standard form). Match the left-hand side with the corresponding right-hand side.  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q12.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q12.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1-P, 2-R, 3-Q, 4-S</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1-Q, 2-R, 3-P, 4-S</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1-Q, 2-S, 3-P, 4-R</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1-R, 2-Q, 3-S, 4-P</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \nabla \cdot \vec{D} &amp;=\rho_{v} \\ \nabla \times \vec{E} &amp;=-\frac{\partial \vec{B}}{\partial t} \\ \nabla \cdot \vec{B} &amp;=0 \\ \nabla \times \vec{H} &amp;=\vec{J}+\frac{\partial \vec{D}}{\partial t} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 40
% Number: Question 40
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">What is the electric flux ([latex]\int \vec{E}\cdot d\hat{a}[/latex]) through a quarter-cylinder of height H (as shown in the figure) due to an infinitely long line charge along the axis of the cylinder with a charge density of Q?  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q11.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q11.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{HQ}{\varepsilon _0}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{HQ}{4\varepsilon _0}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{H\varepsilon _0}{4Q}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4H}{Q\varepsilon _0}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Electric field intensity [latex](\vec{E})[/latex] at '[latex]\rho[/latex]' distance due to infinite long line having line charge density Q is <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \vec{E} &amp;=\frac{Q}{2 \pi \varepsilon_{0} \rho} \hat{a}_{\rho} \\ \int \vec{E} \cdot \vec{da} &amp;=\iint \frac{Q}{2 \pi \varepsilon_{0} \rho} \hat{a}_{p} \cdot \rho d \phi d z \hat{a}_{\rho} \\ &amp;=\frac{Q}{2 \pi \varepsilon_{0}} \int_{&lt;\pi / 2&gt;} d \phi \int_{z=0}^{H} d z \\ &amp;=\frac{Q}{2 \pi \varepsilon_{0}}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right) H=\frac{H Q}{4 \varepsilon_{0}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 41
% Number: Question 41
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A uniform plane wave traveling in free space and having the electric field  <br/> [latex]\vec{E}=(\sqrt{2}\hat{a}_{x}-\hat{a}_{z})cos[6\sqrt{3}\pi \times 10^{8}t-2\pi (x+\sqrt{2}z)]V/m[/latex] <br/> is incident on a dielectric medium (relative permittivity [latex]\gt[/latex]1, relative permeability = 1) as shown in the figure and there is no reflected wave.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q49.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q49.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The relative permittivity (correct to two decimal places) of the dielectric medium is___________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The wave has [latex]E_{i}[/latex] direction and propagation  direction both in same plane (ZX).<br/> The wave is plane of incidence (P) polarized.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \beta_{x} &amp;=1, \quad \beta_{2}=\sqrt{2} \\ \tan \theta_{i} &amp;=\frac{\beta_{z}}{\beta_{x}}=\sqrt{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> No reflection in P polarized wave under Brewster's angle of incidence.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \theta_{i} &amp;=\theta_{B} \\ \tan \theta_{B} &amp;=\sqrt{2}=\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}} \\ \epsilon_{r} &amp;=2 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 42
% Number: Question 42
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The cutoff frequency of [latex] TE_{01} [/latex] mode of an air filled rectangular waveguide having inner
dimensions [latex]a cm \times b cm ( a \gt b)[/latex] is twice that of the dominant [latex] TE_{10}[/latex] mode. When the
waveguide is operated at a frequency which is 25% higher than the cutoff frequency of the
dominant mode, the guide wavelength is found to be 4 cm. The value of b (in cm, correct to
two decimal places) is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} f_{c(01)}=2 f_{c(10)}=\frac{2 c}{2 a}=\frac{c}{a} \\ \frac{c}{2 b}=\frac{c}{a} \Rightarrow a=2 b \Rightarrow b=\frac{a}{2} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Operating frequency,<br/>[latex] f=1.25 f_{c(10)}[/latex]<br/>[latex] f_{c(10)}\lt 1.25 f_{c(10)} \lt \left[f_{c(01)}=2 f_{c(10)}\right][/latex]<br/> So, for the given frequency, the waveguide will work in [latex]\mathrm{TE}_{10}[/latex] mode.<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { So, } \lambda_{g} &amp;=\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{f_{c(10)}}{f}\right)^{2}}} \\ &amp;=\frac{c / f}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{1}{1.25}\right)^{2}}}=\frac{c / f}{0.6} \\ \frac{c}{(1.25) f_{c(10)}(0.6)} &amp;=\lambda_{g}=4 \mathrm{cm} \\ \frac{c}{f_{c(10)}} &amp;=3 \times 10^{-2}=2 a \\ a &amp;=1.5 \mathrm{cm} \\ b &amp;=\frac{a}{2}=0.75 \mathrm{cm} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 43
% Number: Question 43
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The distance (in meters) a wave has to propagate in a medium having a skin depth of 0.1 m
so that the amplitude of the wave attenuates by 20 dB, is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.12</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.23</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.46</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.3</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Attenuation constant,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \alpha &amp;=\frac{1}{\text { skin depth }}=10 \mathrm{Np} / \mathrm{m} \\ \text { 20log }_{10}\left(\frac{E_{0}}{E_{x}}\right) &amp;=20 \mathrm{dB} \\ \frac{E_{o}}{E_{x}} &amp;=10 \Rightarrow E_{x}=\frac{E_{0}}{10} \\ E_{x} &amp;=E_{0} e^{-\alpha x}=E_{0} e^{-10 x}=\frac{E_{0}}{10} \\ e^{-10 x} &amp;=\frac{1}{10} \\ x &amp;=\frac{1}{10} \ln (10)=0.23 \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 44
% Number: Question 44
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A lossy transmission line has resistance per unit length R = 0.05 [latex]\Omega [/latex] /m. The line is
distortionless and has characteristic impedance of 50 [latex]\Omega [/latex]. The attenuation constant (in Np/m, correct to three decimal places) of the line is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For a distortionless transmission line,<br/>[latex] \frac{L}{R}=\frac{C}{G}[/latex]<br/> Propagation constant,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \gamma &amp;=\alpha+j \beta=\sqrt{(R+j \omega L)(G+j \omega C)} \\ &amp;=\sqrt{R G}\left(1+j \omega \frac{L}{R}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Attenuation constant,<br/>[latex] \alpha=\sqrt{R G}[/latex]<br/> Characteristic impedance,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Z_{0} &amp;=\sqrt{\frac{(R+j \omega L)}{(G+j \omega C)}}=\sqrt{\frac{R}{G}} \\ \sqrt{G} &amp;=\frac{\sqrt{R}}{Z_{0}} \\ \text{So,}\quad\alpha &amp;=\sqrt{R} \cdot \frac{\sqrt{R}}{Z_{0}}=\frac{R}{Z_{0}} \\ &amp;=\frac{0.05}{50}=\frac{0.01}{10}=0.001 \mathrm{Np} / \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 45
% Number: Question 45
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The points P, Q, and R shown on the Smith chart (normalized impedance chart) in the
following figure represent: <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q10.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q10.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">P: Short Circuit, Q: Open Circuit, R: Matched Load</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For Short circuit,<br/>[latex] r=x=0 \quad \Rightarrow \text { Point } " P^{\prime \prime}[/latex]<br/> For Open circuit,<br/>[latex] r=x=\infty \quad \Rightarrow \text { Point }^{\prime \prime} R^{\prime \prime}[/latex]<br/> For Matched load,<br/>[latex] r=1 \text { and } x=0 \Rightarrow \text { Point " } Q^{\prime \prime}[/latex]<br/> P: Short Circuit, Q: Matched Load R: Open circuit</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Standard air-filled rectangular waveguides of dimensions a = 2.29 cm and b= 1.02 cm are designed for radar applications. It is desired that these waveguides operate only in the dominatnt [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode but not higher than 95% of the next higher cutoff frequency. The range of the allowable operating frequency f is.</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]8.19 GHz \leq f \leq 13.1 GHz [/latex]</div>

% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]6.55 GHz \leq f \leq 13.1 GHz [/latex]</div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]a=2.29 \;\mathrm{cm}, b=1.02 \mathrm{cm}[/latex]<br/> Waveguide is operating in [latex](TE _{10})[/latex] dominant mode <br/>[latex] f_{c}\left(T E_{10}\right)=\frac{c}{2 a}=\frac{3 \times 10^{10}}{2 \times 2.29}=6.55 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow 25 \% [/latex] above cut-off frequency<br/>[latex] =1.25 \times 6.55 \mathrm{GHz}=8.1875 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex]<br/> Next higher order mode is [latex]\mathrm{TE}_{20}\left(\because a_{2} \gt 2b\right) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{c}\left(T E_{20}\right) &amp;=\frac{c}{a}=\frac{3 \times 10^{10}}{2.29}=13.1 \mathrm{GH}_{2} \\ \Rightarrow 95 \% \text { of } f_{c}\left(\mathrm{TE}_{20}\right) &amp;=0.95 \times 13.1 \\ &amp;=12.445 \mathrm{GHz} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Range of allowable operating frequency<br/>[latex] 8.19 \mathrm{GHz} \leq f \leq 12.45 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 47
% Number: Question 47
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The permittivity of water at optical frequencies is 1.75 [latex]\varepsilon_{0}[/latex]. It is found that an isotropic light source at a distance d under water forms an illuminated circular area of radius 5m, as shown in the figure. The critical angle is [latex]\theta_{c}[/latex].<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q34.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q34.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The value of d (in meter) is _____________</div>

% QUESTION 48
% Number: Question 48
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An electron ([latex]q_1[/latex]) is moving in free space with velocity [latex]10^{5}[/latex] m/s towards a stationary electron ([latex]q_2[/latex]) far away. The closest distance that this moving electron gets to the stationary electron before the repulsive force diverts its path is ___________ [latex]\times 10^{-8}[/latex]m. <br/>[Given, mass of electron [latex]m=9.11 \times 10^{-31}[/latex] kg, charge of electron [latex]e=-1.6 \times 10^{-19}[/latex]C, and permittivity [latex] \varepsilon_{0}=(1/36\pi)\times 10^{-9}F/m][/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/a30.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/a30.jpg"/></noscript><br/>r is the distance at which kinetic energy of [latex]q_{1}[/latex] becomes zero [(because kinetic energy (KE) is converted into potential energy (PE)].<br/> When [latex]q_{1}[/latex] reaches 'r', it starts diverting. <br/> Kinetic energy, [latex]K E=\frac{1}{2} m v^{2}[/latex] and work done in moving [latex]q_{1}[/latex] charge to distance 'r' is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} q_{1} v_{2}&amp;=q_{1} \frac{q_{2}}{4 \pi \varepsilon_{0} r} \\ &amp;\qquad\left(q_{1}=q_{2}=-1.6 \times 10^{-19} \mathrm{C}\right)\\ Now, \quad \frac{1}{2} m v^{2}&amp;=\frac{q_{1} q_{2}}{4 \pi \varepsilon_{0} r} \\ \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow r=\frac{\left(2 \times-1.6 \times 10^{-19}\right) \times\left(-1.6 \times 10^{-19}\right)}{4 \pi \times \frac{10^{-9}}{36 \pi} \times 9.11 \times 10^{-31} \times\left(10^{5}\right)^{2}}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \simeq 5.06 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{m}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 49
% Number: Question 49
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A two - wire transmission line terminates in a television set. The VSWR measured on the line is 5.8. The percentage of power that is reflected from the television set is ______________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given that, VSWR (or) [latex]s=5.8 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s &amp;=\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}=5.8 \\ \Gamma &amp;=\text { Reflection coefficient of } \\ &amp;\quad \text { television set } \\ \Gamma &amp;=\frac{s-1}{s+1}=\frac{4.8}{6.8}=0.70588 \\ \frac{P_{\text {reflected }}}{P_{\text {incident }}} &amp;=\Gamma^{2}=0.4983 \text { or } 49.83 \% \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 50
% Number: Question 50
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Two conducting spheres S1 and S2 of radii a and b (b&gt;a) respectively, are placed far apart and connected by a long, thin conducting wire, as shown in the figure.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q6.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q6.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> For some charge placed on this structure, the potential and surface electric field on S1 are [latex]V_{a}[/latex] and [latex] E_{a}[/latex], and that on S2 are [latex]V_{b}[/latex] and [latex]E_{b}[/latex], respectively, which of the following is CORRECT?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]V_{a}=V_{b} \; and \; E_{a}\lt E_{b}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]V_{a}\gt V_{b} \; and \; E_{a} \gt E_{b}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]V_{a}=V_{b} \; and \;  E_{a} \gt E_{b}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]V_{a}\gt V_{b} \; and \;  E_{a}=E_{b}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/a06.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/a06.jpg"/></noscript><br/>When charge is placed on this structure equilibrium is established such that be  spheres are at same potential i.e.<br/>[latex] V_{a}=V_{b}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c} V_{a}=V_{b} \\ \text { So, } \frac{Q_{a}}{4 \pi \epsilon_{o} a}=\frac{Q_{b}}{4 \pi \epsilon_{o} b} \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{Q_{b}}{Q_{a}}=\frac{b}{a}[/latex]<br/> Now, surface electric fields.<br/>[latex] \frac{E_{a}}{E_{b}}=\left[\frac{Q_{a} / 4 \pi \varepsilon_{o} a^{2}}{Q_{b} / 4 \pi \varepsilon_{o} b^{2}}\right]=\frac{Q_{a} \times b^{2}}{Q_{b} \times a^{2}}=\frac{b}{a}&gt;1[/latex]<br/>[latex] So, E_{a}&gt;E_{b}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 51
% Number: Question 51
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A half wavelength dipole is kept in the x-y plane and oriented along [latex]45^{\circ}[/latex] from the x-axis. Determine the direction of null in the radiation pattern for [latex]0\leq \phi \leq \pi[/latex]. Here the angle [latex]\theta (0\leq \theta \leq \pi)[/latex]
is measured from the z-axis, and the angle [latex]\phi(0\leq \phi \leq 2\pi)[/latex] is measured from the x-axis in the x-y plane.</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\theta =90^{\circ},\; \; \phi =45^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\theta =45^{\circ},\; \; \phi =90^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\theta =90^{\circ},\; \; \phi =135^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\theta =45^{\circ},\; \; \phi =135^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
x-y plane means Z=0 with [latex]Z=r \cos \theta[/latex] (spherical coordinates) [latex]\theta=90^{\circ} [/latex]<br/> With null points along the axis of the dipole it is [latex] \phi=45^{\circ}[/latex] and [latex]225^{\circ}[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20171/a51.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20171/a51.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
