% QUESTION 52
% Number: Question 52
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-1, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The expression for an electric field in free space is [latex]E=E_{0}=(\hat{x}+\hat{y}+j2\hat{z})e^{-j(\omega t-kx+ky)}[/latex], where  x, y,
z represent the spatial coordinates, t represents time, and [latex] \omega[/latex], k are constants. This electric field</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">does not represent a plane wave</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">represents a circular polarized plane wave propagating normal to the z-axis</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">represents an elliptically polarized plane wave propagating along x-y plane.</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">represents a linearly polarized plane wave</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E} [/latex] field direction [latex]\Rightarrow\left(\hat{a}_{x}+\hat{a}_{y}+j 2 \hat{a}_{z}\right)[/latex]<br/> Propagation direction [latex]\Rightarrow k \hat{a}_{x}-k \hat{a}_{y} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \vec{E} [/latex] is perpendicular to propagation<br/>[latex] \vec{E} \cdot \vec{P}=0[/latex]<br/> Component in [latex]\hat{a}_{z}[/latex] has magnitude of 2. <br/> Component in X-Y plane has magnitude of [latex]\sqrt{2}.[/latex]<br/> These two components are out of phase by [latex]90^{\circ} [/latex] and have unequal amplitudes. So, it is elliptically polarized wave.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20171/a44.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20171/a44.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 53
% Number: Question 53
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-1, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An optical fiber is kept along the [latex]\hat{z}[/latex] direction. The refractive indices for the electric fields along [latex]\hat{x}[/latex] and [latex]\hat{y}[/latex] directions in the fiber are [latex]n_{x}[/latex]=1.5000 and [latex]n_{y}[/latex]=1.5001, respectively ([latex]n_{x} \neq n_{y}[/latex] due to the imperfection in the fiber cross-section). The free space wavelength of a light wave propagating in the fiber is 1.5[latex]\mu[/latex]m. If the light wave is circularly polarized at the input of the fiber, the minimum propagation distance after which it becomes linearly polarized, in centimeter, is ___________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Initially the wave is circularly polarized. So, the
initial phase difference between field components
in [latex] \hat{a}_{x} [/latex] direction and [latex] \hat{a}_{y} [/latex] direction is [latex] \frac{\pi}{2} [/latex]<br/>
To become linearly polarized, the wave must travel
a minimum distance, such that, the phase
difference at that point between the field
components in [latex] \hat{a}_{x} [/latex] direction and [latex] \hat{a}_{y} [/latex] direction is [latex] \pi [/latex]<br/>
(i.e., the travel of this minimum distance should
provide an additional phase difference of [latex] \pi / 2 [/latex]
between [latex] \hat{a}_{x} [/latex] and [latex] \hat{a}_{y} [/latex] field components).<br/>So,<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} z_{min}k_z&amp;\sim z_{min}k_y=\frac{\pi}{2} \\ z_{min}\left [ \frac{\omega }{V_{px}}\sim \frac{\omega }{V_{py}} \right ]&amp;= \frac{\pi}{2} \\ 2 \pi z_{min}\left [ \frac{f}{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon _{rx}}\sim \frac{f}{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon _{ry}}\right ]&amp;=\frac{\pi}{2} \\ \frac{4 z_{min}}{\lambda _0}[n_x\sim n_y]&amp;=1 \\ z_{min}&amp;=\frac{\lambda _0}{4(n_x\sim n_y)} \\ &amp;=\frac{1.5}{4(1.5\sim 1.5001)}\mu m \\ &amp;= \frac{1.5}{4(0.0001)}\mu m=\frac{1.5}{4}cm\\ z_{min}&amp;=0.375cm \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 54
% Number: Question 54
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a wireless communication link between a transmitter and a receiver located in free space, with finite and strictly positive capacity. If the effective areas of the transmitter and the receiver antennas, and the distance between them are all doubled, and everything else remains
unchanged, the maximum capacity of the wireless link</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">increases by a factor of 2</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">decreases by a factor of 2</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">remains unchanged</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">decreases by a factor of [latex]\sqrt{2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
As per Friis free space propagation equation,<br/>
[latex]
W_{r}=\frac{W_{t} A_{e r} A_{e t}}{(\lambda d)^{2}}
[/latex]<br/>
when [latex] A_{e r} [/latex] and [latex] A_{e t} [/latex] are doubled and [latex] d [/latex] also doubled, [latex] W_{r} [/latex] is same. Hence capacity is also same.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 55
% Number: Question 55
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-1, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The voltage of an electromagnetic wave propagating in a coaxial cable with uniform characteristic impedance is [latex]V(\iota )=e^{-y \iota +j\omega t}[/latex] volts, Where [latex]\iota [/latex] is the distance along the length of the cable in meters.  [latex] \gamma =(0.1+j40)m^{-1} [/latex] is the complex propagation constant, and [latex] \omega = 2\pi \times 10^{9} rad/ s[/latex]  is the angular frequency. The absolute value of the attenuation in the cable in dB/meter is __________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} V(l) &amp;=V_{O} e^{-\alpha l} e^{-j\beta l} e^{j\omega t} \\ \text { Attenuation } &amp;=\frac{\mid \text { Input } \mid}{\mid \text { Output } \mid}=\frac{\left|V_{O}(0)\right|}{\left|V_{o}(l)\right|} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Attenuation per meter [latex]=\frac{\left|V_{0}\right|}{\left|V_{0}(1 \mathrm{m})\right|}=e^{\alpha} [/latex]<br/> Attenuation in [latex]\mathrm{dB} / \mathrm{m}=\left(20 \log e^{\alpha}\right) \mathrm{dB} / \mathrm{m} [/latex]<br/>[latex] =20(0.1) \log e=0.868 \mathrm{dB} / \mathrm{m}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 56
% Number: Question 56
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the charge profile shown in the figure. The resultant potential distribution is best described by <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q55.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q55.jpg"/></noscript><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q55a.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q55a.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Applying Poisson's equations<br/>[latex] \nabla^{2} V=\frac{\partial^{2} V}{\partial x^{2}}=-\frac{\rho_{v}}{\epsilon}=K[/latex]<br/> Constant charge density<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{\partial V}{\partial x} &amp;=-K x+K^{\prime} \\ V &amp;=\frac{-K x^{2}}{2}+K^{\prime} x+K^{\prime} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Towards positive x or negative side.<br/> It is a second order parabolic increase.<br/> Due to symmetry of + and - charges K" = 0 is expected with V = O at centre and graph passing through origin.<br/> Beyond [latex]x \gt 0 \text{ or } x \lt  b, \; E = 0[/latex] due to capacitive nature of + and - charges<br/>[latex] V=-\int 0 \cdot \overrightarrow{d l}=\text { Constant }[/latex]<br/> This  constant is same V at x=a or x=b</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 57
% Number: Question 57
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A radar operating at 5 GHz uses a common antenna for transmission and reception. The antenna has a gain of 150 and is aligned for maximum directional radiation and reception to a target 1 km away having radar cross-section of 3 [latex]m^{2}[/latex]. If it transmits 100 kW, then the received power (in [latex]\mu[/latex]W) is__________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} f&amp;=5 \mathrm{GHz}, G=150, R=1 \mathrm{km} \\ P_{t}&amp;=100 \mathrm{kW}, \sigma=3 \mathrm{m}^{2} \\ P_{r}&amp;= \frac{(G \lambda)^{2} \cdot \sigma}{(4 \pi)^{3} R^{4}} \times P_{t} \\ &amp;=\frac{\left(150 \times \frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{5 \times 10^{9}}\right)^{2} \cdot 3}{(4 \pi)^{3} \cdot 10^{12}} \times 10^{5} \\ &amp;=\frac{81 \times 3 \times 10^{5}}{(4 \pi)^{3} \times 10^{12}} \\ &amp;= 1.224 \times 10^{-8}=0.012 \mu \mathrm{W} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 58
% Number: Question 58
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider an air-filled rectangular waveguide with dimensions a = 2.286 cm and b = 1.016 cm. The increasing order of the cut-off frequencies for different modes is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]TE_{01} \lt TE_{10} \lt TE_{11} \lt TE_{20}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]TE_{20} \lt TE_{11} \lt TE_{10} \lt TE_{01}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]TE_{10} \lt TE_{20} \lt TE_{01} \lt TE_{11}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]TE_{10} \lt TE_{11} \lt TE_{20} \lt TE_{01}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} a &amp;=2.286 \mathrm{cm} \\ b &amp;=1.016 \mathrm{cm} \\ f_{c_{T E_{10}}} &amp;=\frac{c}{2 a} \\ f_{c_{T E_{01}}} &amp;=\frac{c}{2 b} \\ f_{c_{T E_{20}}} &amp;=\frac{c}{a} \\ f_{c_{T E_{11}}} &amp;=\frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2}} \\ \text{As }a \gt 2 b \Rightarrow f_{c_{T E_{10}}} &amp; \lt f_{c_{T E_{20}}} \lt f_{c_{T E_{01}}} \lt f_{c_{T E_{11}}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 59
% Number: Question 59
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider an air-filled rectangular waveguide with dimensions a = 2.286 cm and b = 1.016 cm. At 10 GHz operating frequency, the value of the propagation constant (per meter) of the corresponding propagating mode is __________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Operation frequency [latex]=10 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex]<br/> Cut off frequency for [latex]\mathrm{TE}_{10}, \mathrm{f}_{c}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{2 \mathrm{a}} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{c} &amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2 \times 2.286 \times 10^{-2}} \\ &amp;=6.57 \mathrm{GHz} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Next mode [latex]T E_{20}[/latex] has [latex]f_{c}=13 \mathrm{GHz} [/latex]<br/>[latex] 10 \mathrm{GHz} [/latex] operates only in dominant mode.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} r &amp;=\sqrt{\left(\frac{m \pi}{a}\right)^{2}-\omega^{2} \mu \epsilon} \\ &amp;=\sqrt{\left(\frac{\pi}{a}\right)^{2}-\frac{(2 \pi f)^{2}}{\left(3 \times 10^{8}\right)^{2}}} \\ &amp;=\pi \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{2.286 \times 10^{-2}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{2 \times 10 \times 10^{9}}{3 \times 10^{8}}\right)^{2}} \\ &amp;=j 158\left(\mathrm{m}^{-1}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 62
% Number: Question 62
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A positive charge q is placed at x= 0 between two infinite metal plates placed at x =-d and at x = +d respectively. The metal plates lie in the yz plane.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q55.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q55.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The charge is at rest at t = 0, when a voltage +V is applied to the plate at -d and voltage -V is
applied to the plate at x=+d . Assume that the quantity of the charge q is small enough that it does not perturb the field set up by the metal plates. The time that the charge q takes to reach the right plate is proportional to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">d/V</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{d}/V[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]d/ \sqrt{V}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{d/V}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Velocity being free velocity,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{1}{2} m v^{2} &amp;=q V \\ v &amp;=\frac{d}{t}=\sqrt{\frac{2 q V}{m}} \\ \Rightarrow t \propto \frac{d}{\sqrt{V}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 63
% Number: Question 63
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A microwave circuit consisting of lossless transmission lines [latex]T_{1} \; and \;  T_{2}[/latex] is shown in the figure. The plot shows the magnitude of the input reflection coefficient  [latex]\tau[/latex] as a function of frequency f. The phase velocity of the signal in the transmission lines is [latex]2*10^{8} m/s[/latex]. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q54.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> The length L (in meters) of [latex]T_2[/latex] is _______</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
At frequency of [latex]1 \mathrm{GHz}[/latex] the line is matched and [latex] | \Gamma |=0 [/latex] as seen in the graph.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/a54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/a54.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/> The line [latex]T_{1}[/latex] is having [latex]50 \Omega[/latex] load parallel to [latex]T_{2}[/latex] input impedance. <br/> This load is [latex]50\Omega[/latex] when [latex]T_{2}[/latex] input impedance is infinite. <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Z_{\text {in } 2}&amp;=\infty=Z_{L 2} \text{ (open circuit) }\\ T_{2}\text{ has to be a }\frac{\lambda}{2}\text{ line}.&amp;\\ f &amp;=1 \mathrm{GHz} \\ \lambda &amp;=\frac{2 \times 10^{10}}{1 \times 10^{9}} \mathrm{cm} \\ \frac{\lambda}{2} &amp;=L=10 \mathrm{cm}=0.1 \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 64
% Number: Question 64
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A lossless microstrip transmission line consists of a trace of width w. It is drawn over a practically infinite ground plane and is separated by a dielectric slab of thickness t and relative permittivity [latex]\varepsilon _{r} \gt 1[/latex].  The inductance per unit length and the characteristic impedance of this line are L and [latex]Z_{0}[/latex], respectively.  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q53.jpg"/></noscript><br/> Which one of the following inequalities is always satisfied?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]Z_{0} \gt \sqrt{\frac{Lt}{\varepsilon _{0}\varepsilon_{r}w }}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]Z_{0} \lt \sqrt{\frac{Lt}{\varepsilon _{0}\varepsilon_{r} w}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]Z_{0} \gt\sqrt{\frac{Lw}{\varepsilon _{0}\varepsilon_{r}t }}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]Z_{0} \lt \sqrt{\frac{Lw}{\varepsilon _{0}\varepsilon_{r}t }}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 65
% Number: Question 65
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The parallel-plate capacitor shown in the figure has movable plates. The capacitor is charged so that the energy stored in it is E when the plate separation is d. The capacitor is then isolated electrically and the plates are moved such that the plate separation becomes 2d. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q52.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q52.jpg"/></noscript><br/> At this new plate separation, what is the energy stored in the capacitor, neglecting fringing effects?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2E</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{2}E[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">E</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">E/2</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \text { Let, } E=E_{1} \text { , Energy } E_{1}=\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{2 C_{1}} \\ \text { Electrically isolated } \Rightarrow Q_{2}=Q_{1} \\ \begin{aligned} d_{2}=2 d_{1} \Rightarrow \;&amp; C_{2}=\frac{C_{1}}{2} \\ E_{2} &amp;=\frac{Q_{2}^{2}}{2 C_{2}}=\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{\frac{2 C_{1}}{2}}=2\left(\frac{Q_{1}^{2}}{2 C_{1}}\right) \\ &amp;=2 E_{1}=2 E \end{aligned} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 66
% Number: Question 66
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Light from free space is incident at an angle [latex]\theta _{i}[/latex]  to the normal of the facet of a step-index large core optical fibre. The core and cladding refractive indices are [latex]n _{1}=1.5 \; and \; n_{2}=1.4[/latex], respectively.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q25.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q25.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The maximum value of [latex]\theta _{i}[/latex] (in degrees) for which the incident light will be guided in the core of the fibre is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} &amp; \sin \alpha_{\max } &amp;=\sqrt{n_{1}^{2}-n_{2}^{2}}=\sqrt{1.5^{2}-1.4^{2}} \\ \Rightarrow &amp; \alpha_{\max } &amp;=\sin ^{-1}(0.5385)=32.58^{\circ} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 67
% Number: Question 67
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Let the electric field vector of a plane electromagnetic wave propagating in a homogenous medium be expressed as [latex]E=\hat{x}E_{x}e^{-j(\omega t-\beta z)}[/latex], where the propagation constant [latex]\beta[/latex] is a function of the angular frequency [latex]\omega[/latex]. Assume that [latex]\beta (\omega)[/latex]
and [latex]E_{x}[/latex] are known and are real. From the information available, which one of the following CANNOT be determined?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The type of polarization of the wave.</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The group velocity of the wave</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The phase velocity of the wave.</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">The power flux through the z = 0 plane.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\beta(\omega) [/latex] is known so [latex] V_{g}[/latex] can be calculated. <br/>[latex] v_{p}=\omega / \beta [/latex] can be calculated.<br/> Polarization can be identified.<br/>[latex] \mu_{r}[/latex] and [latex]\epsilon_{r} [/latex] cannot be found, due to which power flux cannot be calculated as power flux <br/>[latex] P=\frac{1}{2} \frac{|E|^{2}}{\eta}, \text{ where }\eta=120 \pi \times \sqrt{\frac{\mu_{r}}{\epsilon_{r}}}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 68
% Number: Question 68
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A uniform and constant magnetic field [latex]B=\hat{z}B[/latex] exists in the [latex]\hat{z}[/latex] direction in vacuum. A particle of mass m with a small charge q is introduced into this region with an initial velocity [latex]v=\hat{x}v_{x}+\hat{z}v_{z}[/latex]. Given that B, m, q, [latex]v_{x}[/latex] and [latex]v_{z}[/latex] are all non-zero, which one of the following describes the eventual trajectory of the particle?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Helical motion in the [latex]\hat{z}[/latex] direction</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Circular motion in the xy plane</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linear motion in the [latex]\hat{z}[/latex] direction</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linear motion in the [latex]\hat{x}[/latex] direction</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\mathrm{Ba}_{\mathrm{z}}[/latex] magnetic field <br/>[latex] v_{x} a_{x}+v_{z} a_{z}[/latex] velocity <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} F&amp;=Q(v \times B) \text{ by Lorentz's law}\\ &amp;=Q\left(v_{x} a_{x}+v_{z} a_{z}\right) \times B a_{z} \\ F_{y} &amp;=Q v_{x} \cdot B\left(-a_{y}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> This results in a circular path in the XY plane with  [latex]v_{z} a_{z}[/latex] component causing a linear path. <br/> Both result in a helical path along Z axis.</div>

% QUESTION 69
% Number: Question 69
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The far-zone power density radiated by a helical antenna is approximated as: <br/><br/>[latex]\vec{W}_{rad}=\vec{W}_{average}\approx \hat{a}_{r}C_{o}\frac{1}{r^{2}}cos^{4}\theta[/latex]<br/><br/>  The radiated power density is symmetrical with respect to [latex]\phi[/latex] and exists only in the upper hemisphere: [latex]0\leq \theta \leq \frac{\pi }{2};0\leq \phi \leq 2\pi;C_{o}[/latex] is a constant. The power radiated by the antenna (in watts) and the maximum directivity of the antenna, respectively, are</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.5C_{o},10dB[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.256C_{o},10dB[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.256C_{o},12dB[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.5C_{o},12dB[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { Power radiated }&amp;=\int P_{\mathrm{rad}} \cdot d s \\ =&amp; \int_{\theta=0}^{\pi / 2} \int_{\theta=0}^{2 \pi} C_{0} \frac{1}{r^{2}} \cos ^{4} \theta \cdot r^{2} \sin \theta d \theta d \phi \\ =&amp; 2 \pi \cdot \frac{C_{0}}{r^{2}} \cdot r^{2} \int_{\theta=0}^{\pi / 2} \cos ^{4} \theta \cdot d(-\cos \theta) \\ =&amp;\left.2 \pi \cdot C_{0}\left(-\frac{\cos ^{5} \theta}{5}\right)\right|_{0} ^{\pi / 2} \\ =&amp; \frac{2 \pi}{5} C_{0}=1.256 C_{0} \\ \text{Directivity}=&amp; \frac{4 \pi \cdot U(\theta, \phi)}{\int(\theta, \phi) \cdot d \Omega} \\ =&amp; \frac{4 \pi \cdot C_{0} \cdot \cos ^{4} \theta}{\int_{\theta=0}^{\pi / 2}\int_{\phi=0}^{2\pi}C_{0} \cdot \cos ^{4} \theta \cdot \sin \theta d \theta d \phi} \\ &amp;=\frac{2 \cos ^{5} \theta}{1 / 5}=10 \cos ^{5} \theta \\ \max _{\operatorname{value}}=&amp; 10 \\ \Rightarrow \max \text { value }(\text { in } \mathrm{dB}) &amp;=10 \log _{10} 10=10 \mathrm{dB} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 70
% Number: Question 70
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The electric field of a uniform plane wave travelling along the negative z direction is given by the following equation: <br/><br/>[latex]\vec{E}^{1}_{w}=(\hat{a}_{x}+j\hat{a}_{y})E_{0}e^{jkz}[/latex] <br/><br/>This wave is incident upon a receiving antenna placed at the origin and whose radiated electric field towards the incident wave is given by the following equation:  <br/><br/>[latex]\vec{E}_{a}=(\hat{a}_{x}+2\hat{a}_{y})E_{I}\frac{1}{r}e^{-jkr}[/latex]    <br/><br/>The polarization of the incident wave, the polarization of the antenna and losses due to the polarization mismatch are, respectively,</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linear, Circular (clockwise), -5dB</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Circular (clockwise), Linear, -5dB</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Circular (clockwise), Linear, -3dB</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Circular (anti clockwise), Linear, -3dB</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E}_{T}=\left(\hat{a}_{x}+j \hat{a}_{y}\right) E_{0} e^{j k z} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow [/latex] Wave contains two orthogonal components and  Y component leads X component leads by [latex]90^{\circ} [/latex] and also wave is travelling in negative z-direction. <br/>[latex] \vec{E}_{R}=\left(\hat{a}_{x}+2 \hat{a}_{y}\right) E_{1} \frac{1}{r} e^{-j k r} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow [/latex] Wave contains two orthogonal components  with unequal amplitudes and both are in-phase.<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow [/latex] Linear polarization.<br/> Polarization Loss Factor, [latex] \mathrm{PLF}=\left|\hat{E}_{T} \cdot \hat{E}_{R}\right|^{2} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Where, E_{T}&amp;=\frac{\hat{a}_{x}+j \hat{a}_{y}}{\sqrt{2}} \\ E_{R}&amp;=\frac{\hat{a}_{x}+2 \hat{a}_{y}}{\sqrt{5}} \\ P L&amp;= \left|\frac{1+j 2^{2}}{\sqrt{10} }\right|=\frac{5}{10}=\frac{1}{2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad P L F(d B)&amp;=10 \log \frac{1}{2}=-3 \mathrm{dB} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 71
% Number: Question 71
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Two lossless X-band horn antennas are separated by a distance of 200[latex]\lambda[/latex]. The amplitude reflection coefficients at the terminals of the transmitting and receiving antennas are 0.15 and 0.18, respectively. The maximum directivities of the transmitting and receiving antennas (over the isotropic antenna) are 18 dB and 22 dB, respectively. Assuming that the input power in the lossless transmission line connected to the antenna is 2 W, and that the antennas are perfectly aligned and
polarization matched, the power ( in mW) delivered to the load at the receiver is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20161/a53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20161/a53.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} G_{t}&amp;=10^{1.8}, G_{r}=10^{2.2} \\ P_{r}&amp;=\frac{\left(1-\left|\Gamma_{t}\right|^{2}\right)\left(1-\left|\Gamma_{r}\right|^{2}\right) G_{t} G_{r}}{\left(\frac{4 \pi d}{\lambda}\right)^{2}} \cdot P_{t}\\ &amp;=\frac{\left(1-|0.15|^{2}\right)\left(1-|0.18|^{2}\right) 10^{1.8} \cdot 10^{2.2}}{\left(\frac{4 \pi 200 \lambda}{\lambda}\right)^{2}} \times 2 \\ &amp;=2.99476 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{W}=2.995 \mathrm{mW} \approx 3 \mathrm{mW} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 72
% Number: Question 72
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The current density in a medium is given by <br/> [latex]\vec{j}=\frac{400 sin\theta }{2\pi (r^{2})+4}\hat{a}_{r}Am^{-2}[/latex]<br/> The total current and the average current density flowing through the portion of a spherical surface r = 0.8 m, [latex]\frac{\pi }{12}\leq \theta \leq \frac{\pi }{4},0\leq \phi \leq 2\pi[/latex] are given, respectively, by</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">15.09 A, 12.86 [latex]Am^{-2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">18.73 A, 13.65 [latex]Am^{-2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">12.86 A, 9.23 [latex]Am^{-2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">10.28 A, 7.56 [latex]Am^{-2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} I &amp;=\int \overrightarrow{J} \cdot \overrightarrow{d s} \\ &amp;=\int_{\theta=\frac{\pi}{12}}^{\pi / 4} \int_{\phi=0}^{2 \pi} \frac{400 \sin \theta}{2 \pi\left(r^{2}+4\right)} r^{2} \sin \theta d \theta d \phi \\ &amp;=\left.\frac{400}{2 \pi\left(r^{2}+4\right)} \cdot r^{2} \cdot \phi\right|_{0} ^{2 \pi} \int_{\pi / 12}^{\pi / 4} \sin ^{2} \theta d \theta \\ &amp;=\frac{400 r^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+4\right)} \int_{\pi / 12}^{\pi / 4}\left(\frac{1-\cos 2 \theta}{2}\right) d \theta \\ &amp;=\frac{400 \cdot r^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+4\right)}\left(\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{4}-\frac{\pi}{12}\right)}{2}-\left(\frac{\sin 2 \theta}{4}\right)_{\pi / 12}^{\pi / 4}\right) \\ &amp;=\left.\frac{400 \cdot r^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+4\right)}\left(\frac{\pi}{12}-\left(\frac{1-1 / 2}{4}\right)\right)\right|_{r=0.8} \\ &amp;=\frac{400 \times 0.8 \times 0.8}{4.64} \times 0.13=7.17 \mathrm{Amp} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex]\begin{array}{l} \text { Total area }=\int d s=\iint r^{2} \sin \theta d \theta d \phi \\ =r^{2} \int_{0=\frac{\pi}{12}}^{\pi / 4} \sin \theta d \theta \cdot 2 \pi \\ =\left.r^{2} \cdot 2 \pi \cdot 0.259\right|_{r=0.8} \\ =0.8^{2} \times 0.5 \times 2 \pi \times \frac{1}{4} \\ =1.041 \mathrm{m}^{2} \\ \text { Average current }=\frac{7.17}{1.041}=6.88 \mathrm{A} / \mathrm{m}^{2} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Note : GATE key mentioned (MTA - marks to all)</div>

% QUESTION 73
% Number: Question 73
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The propagation constant of a lossy transmission line is [latex] (2 +j5) m^{-1}[/latex]
and its characteristic impedance is [latex](50 + j0) \Omega[/latex] at [latex]\omega = 10^{6}[/latex] rad [latex]s^{-1}[/latex]. The values of the line constants L, C,R, G are, respectively,</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]L=200\mu H/m, C=0.1 \mu F/m,[/latex] [latex]R=50\Omega /m, G=0.02 S/m[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]L=250\mu H/m, C=0.1 \mu F/m,[/latex] [latex]R=100\Omega /m, G=0.04 S/m[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]L=200\mu H/m, C=0.2 \mu F/m,[/latex] [latex]R=100\Omega /m, G=0.04 S/m[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]L=250\mu H/m, C=0.2 \mu F/m,[/latex] [latex] R=50\Omega /m, G=0.04 S/m[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \gamma &amp;=\sqrt{(R+j \omega L)(G+j \omega C)} \\ Z_{0} &amp;=\sqrt{\frac{R+j \omega L}{G+j \omega C}} \\ \gamma \cdot Z_{0}=R+j \omega L =&amp;(2+j 5)(50+j 0)=100+j 250 \\ R &amp;=100 \Omega / m \\ L &amp;=\frac{250}{\omega}=\frac{250}{10^{6}}=250 \mu \mathrm{H} / \mathrm{m} \\ \frac{\gamma}{Z_{0}}=\frac{2+j 5}{50}=G &amp;+\dot{j \omega C}=0.04+j 0.1 \\ G &amp;=0.04 \mathrm{S} / \mathrm{m} \\ C &amp;=\frac{0.1}{\omega}=\frac{0.1}{10^{6}}=0.1 \mu \mathrm{F} / \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 74
% Number: Question 74
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Concentric spherical shells of radii 2 m, 4 m, and 8 m carry uniform surface charge densities of 20 nC/[latex]m^{2}[/latex], -4 nC/[latex]m^{2}[/latex] and [latex]\rho _{s}[/latex] , respectively. The value of [latex]\rho _{s}  (nC/m^{2})[/latex] required to ensure that the
electric flux density [latex]\overrightarrow{D}=\overrightarrow{0}[/latex]at radius 10 m is _________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\oint \vec{D} \cdot ds=Q \quad [/latex] (charge enclosed) <br/>[latex] Q_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{3}=0 [/latex]<br/> For D=0 <br/>[latex] \rho_{s 1} \cdot 4 \pi 2^{2}+\rho_{s 2} \cdot 4 \pi \cdot 4^{2}+\rho_{s 3} \cdot 4 \pi \cdot 8^{2}=Q=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] 20 \cdot 4-4.4^{2}+\rho_{s 3} \cdot 8^{2}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] 80-64+\rho_{s 3} \cdot 8^{2}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \rho_{s 3}=\frac{-16}{64}=-0.25 \mathrm{nC} / \mathrm{m}^{2}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 75
% Number: Question 75
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A coaxial capacitor of inner radius 1 mm and outer radius 5 mm has a capacitance per unit length of 172 pF/m. If the ratio of outer radius to inner radius is doubled, the capacitance per unit length (in pF/m) is ________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} C &amp;=\frac{2 \pi \in}{ln \frac{b}{a}} \\ 172 \times 10^{-12} &amp;=\frac{2 \pi \epsilon}{ln 5} \\ &amp; \in \frac{172 \times 10^{-12} \times ln 5}{2 \pi} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> If ratio of [latex]\frac{b}{a} [/latex] is doubled then<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} C=\frac{2 \pi \epsilon}{\ln 10}=\frac{2 \pi \times \frac{172 \times 10^{-12} \times \ln 5}{2 \pi}}{\ln 10} \\ C=120.22 \mathrm{pF} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 76
% Number: Question 76
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A 200 m long transmission line having parameters shown in the figure is terminated into a load [latex]R_{L}[/latex]. The line is connected to a 400 V source having source resistance [latex]R_{S}[/latex] through a switch, which is closed at t = 0. The transient response of the circuit at the input of the line (z = 0) is also drawn in the figure. The value of [latex]R_{L}[/latex] (in [latex] \Omega [/latex]) is ________.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q54.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { At } t&amp;=0 \\ &amp;V_{\text {in }}=400 \times \frac{R_{0}}{R_{0}+R_{s}}=400 \times \frac{50}{200}=100 V \\ \text { At } t&amp;=T_{0} \Gamma_{L} 100 \text { volts reflects }\\ \text { At } t&amp;=2T_{0}\\ V_{\text {in }} &amp;=100+\left(1+\Gamma_{\text {in }}\right) \Gamma_{L} 100=62.5 \\ \Gamma_{\text {in }} &amp;=\frac{R_{s}-R_{o}}{R_{s}+R_{o}}=\frac{1}{2} \\ \text { Therefore, } \Gamma_{L} &amp;=-\frac{1}{4}=\frac{R_{L}-50}{R_{L}+50} \\ R_{L} &amp;=30 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 77
% Number: Question 77
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the 3 m long lossless air-filled transmission line shown in the figure. It has a characteristic impedance of 120[latex]\pi \Omega[/latex], is terminated by a short circuit, and is excited with a frequency of 37.5 MHz. What is the nature of the input impedance ([latex]Z_{in}[/latex])?<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q53.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Open</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Short</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Inductive</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Capacitive</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} l &amp;=3 \mathrm{m} \\ f &amp;=37.5 \mathrm{MHz} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \lambda &amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{37.5 \times 10^{6}}=8 \mathrm{m} \\ Z_{\text {in }}(S C) &amp;=j Z_{0} \tan \beta l=j 120 \pi \tan \frac{2 \pi}{8} \cdot 3 \\ &amp;=-j 120 \pi \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 78
% Number: Question 78
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A vector field [latex]D=2\rho ^{2} a_{\rho }+z a_{z}[/latex] exists inside a cylindrical region enclosed by the surfaces [latex]\rho[/latex]= 1, z = 0 and z = 5. Let S be the surface bounding this cylindrical region. The surface integral of this field on [latex]S(\oint\oint_{S} D.ds)[/latex]  is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{c} \vec{D}=2 \rho^{2} \hat{a}_{p}+z \hat{a}_{z} \\ \text { surface: } \rho=1, z=0 \text { and } z=5 \\ \oiint_{s} \vec{D} \cdot ds=\int_{\text {Top surface}} \vec{D} \cdot ds \\ +\int_{\text {Bottom surface}} \vec{D} \cdot ds \\ +\int_{\text {curved surface }} \vec{D} \cdot ds \\ =5 \pi+0+20 \pi=25 \pi=78.54 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 80
% Number: Question 80
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The directivity of an antenna array can be increased by adding more antenna elements, as a larger number of elements</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">improves the radiation efficiency</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">increases the effective area of the antenna</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">results in a better impedance matching</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">allows more power to be transmitted by the antenna</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Directivity (0) is increased by adding more antenna elements in an antenna array. Effective area [latex](A_{e})[/latex] and directivity (D) are related by <br/>[latex] A_{e}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4 \pi} D [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow D \uparrow A_{e} \uparrow[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 81
% Number: Question 81
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The electric field of a plane wave propagating in a lossless non-magnetic medium is given by the following expression <br/>[latex]E(z,t)=a_{x}5 cos(2\pi \times 10^{9}t+\beta z)+ [/latex] [latex]a_{y} 3 cos(2\pi \times 10^{9}t+\beta z-\pi/2)[/latex] <br/> The type of the polarization is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Right Hand Circular.</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Left Hand Elliptical</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Right Hand Elliptical</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Linear.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E}(z, t)=\hat{a}_{x} 5 \cos \left(2 \pi \times 10^{9} t+\beta z\right) [/latex]<br/>[latex] +\hat{a}_{y} 3 \cos \left(2 \pi \times 10^{9} t+\beta z-\frac{\pi}{2}\right) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow [/latex] Wave is travelling in [latex]-\hat{a}_{z}[/latex] direction.<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow [/latex] Wave has orthogonal components with unequal amplitudes and checking the time trace.<br/>[latex] \therefore[/latex] Wave is left hand elliptically polarized.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 82
% Number: Question 82
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Two half-wave dipole antennas placed as shown in the figure are excited with sinusoidally varying currents of frequency 3 MHz and phase shift of [latex]\pi/2[/latex] between them(the element at the origin leads in phase). If the maximum radiated E-field at the point P in the x-y plane occurs at an azimuthal angle of [latex]60^{\circ}[/latex], the distance d (in meters) between the antennas is _________. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q54.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Both dipole antennas have isotropic pattern in [latex]\phi [/latex] view or H plane view.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/a54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/a54.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>[latex] \psi=\beta d \cos \theta+\alpha[/latex]<br/> where, [latex]\alpha=-90^{\circ}, \beta=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda}=\frac{2 \pi}{\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{3 \times 10^{6}}}=\frac{2 \pi}{100} [/latex]<br/> Since maximum radiation [latex](\psi=0)[/latex] is at [latex]\theta=60^{\circ} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \Rightarrow \quad 0=\frac{2 \pi}{100} d \cos 60^{\circ}-90^{\circ} \\ \Rightarrow \quad 90=\frac{360}{100} d \frac{1}{2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad d=50 \mathrm{m} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 83
% Number: Question 83
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An air-filled rectangular waveguide of internal dimensions [latex]a \; cm \times b \;  cm \; (a \gt b)[/latex] has a cutoff frequency of 6 GHz for the dominant [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode. For the same waveguide, if the cutoff frequencyof the [latex] TM_{11}[/latex] mode is 15 GHz, the cutoff frequency of the [latex]TE_{01}[/latex] mode in GHz is __________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given, [latex]f_{c} T E_{10}[/latex] mode is [latex]6 \mathrm{GHz} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \quad \frac{c}{2 a}=\frac{3 \times 10^{10}}{2 \times a}=6 \times 10^{9} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow a=2.5 \mathrm{cm} [/latex]<br/> Given [latex]f_{c}[/latex] for [latex]T M_{11}[/latex] mode is [latex]15 \mathrm{GHz} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \Rightarrow \frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\frac{1}{a^{2}}+\frac{1}{b^{2}}}=15 \times 10^{9} \\ \begin{array}{l} \frac{3 \times 10^{10}}{2} \sqrt{\frac{1}{6.25}+\frac{1}{b^{2}}}=15 \times 10^{9} \\ \Rightarrow \quad b=\frac{2.5}{\sqrt{5.25}} \end{array} \\ \begin{aligned} \Rightarrow \quad &amp; \quad 2 \end{aligned} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Now [latex]f_{c}[/latex] for [latex]T E_{01}[/latex] mode is,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{c} &amp;=\frac{c}{2 b}=\frac{3 \times 10^{10}}{2.5} \sqrt{5.25} \\ &amp;=13.75 \mathrm{GHz} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 85
% Number: Question 85
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In a source free region in vacuum, if the electrostatic potential [latex]\varphi = 2x^{2}+y^{2}+cz^{2}[/latex], the value of constant c must be _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\phi=2 x^{2}+y^{2}+c z^{2}[/latex]<br/> In source-free region, <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \nabla \cdot \vec{D}&amp;=0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \in(\nabla \cdot \vec{E}) &amp;=0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \nabla \cdot \vec{E} &amp;=0\\ where,\quad\vec{E}&amp;=-\nabla V=-\nabla \phi \\ Now, \quad \nabla V&amp;=\frac{\partial \phi}{\partial x} \hat{a}_{x}+\frac{\partial \phi}{\partial y} \hat{a}_{y}+\frac{\partial \phi}{\partial z} \hat{a}_{z} \\ \Rightarrow \quad -\vec{E}&amp;=4 x \hat{a}_{x}+2 y \hat{a}_{y}+2 c z \hat{a}_{z} \\ \text{Again }\nabla \cdot \vec{E}&amp;=0 \\ \Rightarrow \frac{\partial E_{x}}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial E_{y}}{\partial y}&amp;+\frac{\partial E_{t}}{\partial z}=4+2+2 c=0 \\ c&amp;=-3 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 86
% Number: Question 86
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a uniform plane wave with amplitude [latex](E_{0})[/latex] of 10 V/m and 1.1 GHz frequency travelling in air, and incident normally on a dielectric medium with complex relative permittivity ([latex]\varepsilon_{r}[/latex]) and permeability ([latex]\mu_{r}[/latex]) as shown in the figure.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/q55.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/q55.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
The magnitude of the transmitted electric field component (in V/m) after it has travelled a distance of 10 cm inside the dielectric region is_________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Material has [latex]\mu_{R}[/latex] and [latex]\epsilon_{R}[/latex] complex.<br/>[latex] \eta \text { of material }=\sqrt{\frac{j \omega \mu}{\sigma+j \omega \epsilon}}=\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{o} \mu_{R}}{\epsilon_{o} \epsilon_{R}}}=120 \pi[/latex]<br/> where, [latex]\sigma=0 \quad[/latex] (dielectric) <br/> No reflections at the boundary.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \gamma &amp;=\text { Propagation constant } \\ &amp;=\sqrt{j \omega \mu_{o} \mu_{R}\left(0+j \omega \epsilon_{o} \epsilon_{R}\right)} \\ &amp;=\sqrt{j \omega \mu_{o} \epsilon_{o}(1-j 2)} \\ &amp;=j \omega \sqrt{\mu_{o} \epsilon_{o}}+j 2 \omega \sqrt{\mu_{o} \epsilon_{o}} \\ \alpha &amp;=2 \omega \sqrt{\mu_{o} \epsilon_{o}}=4.6 \frac{1}{\mathrm{m}} \\ |E| &amp;=E_{o} e^{-\alpha z} \text { with } Z=0.1 \mathrm{m} \\ |E| &amp;=0.1 \mathrm{V} / \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 87
% Number: Question 87
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The electric field intensity of a plane wave traveling in free space is given by the following
expression <br/> [latex]E(x,t)=a_{y}24\pi cos(\omega t-k_{0}x)(V/m)[/latex]  <br/> In this field, consider a square area 10 cm x 10 cm on a plane x + y = 1. The total time-averaged power (in mW) passing through the square area is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E}(x, t)=\hat{a}_{y} 24 \pi \cos \left(\omega t-K_{o} x\right) V / m[/latex]<br/> Power density<br/>[latex] \vec{P}=\frac{1}{2} \frac{|E|^{2}}{\eta}=\frac{1}{2} \frac{(24 \pi)^{2}}{120 \pi} \hat{a}_{x}[/latex]<br/> Time-averaged power<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P_{\mathrm{avg}} &amp;=\vec{P} \cdot \overrightarrow{d s} \\ &amp;=\left[\frac{1}{2} \frac{(24 \pi)^{2}}{120 \pi} \hat{a}_{x}\right] \cdot\left[\frac{\hat{a}_{x}+\hat{a}_{y}}{\sqrt{2}} \times 10^{-2}\right] \\ P_{\mathrm{avg}} &amp;=\frac{24 \pi}{\sqrt{2}}=53.29 \mathrm{mW} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">

% QUESTION 91
% Number: Question 91
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">If [latex]\vec{E}=-9(2y^{3}-3yz^{2})\hat{x}-(6xy^{2}-3xz^{2})\hat{y}+(6xyz)\hat{z}[/latex] is the electric field in a source free region, a valid expression for the electrostatic potential is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]xy^{3}-yz^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2xy^{3}-xyz^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]y^{3}+xyz^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]2xy^{3}-3xyz^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} E &amp;=-\frac{\partial V}{\partial x} \hat{a}_{x}-\frac{\partial V}{\partial y} \hat{a}_{y}-\frac{\partial V}{\partial Z} \hat{a}_{z} \\ \frac{\partial V}{\partial x} &amp;=2 y^{3}-3 y z^{2} &amp;V=2 x y^{3}-3 x y z^{2}+K_{1} \\ \frac{\partial V}{\partial y} &amp;=6 x y^{2}-3 x z^{2} &amp;V=2 x y^{3}-3 x y z^{2}+K_{2} \\ \frac{\partial V}{\partial z} &amp;=-6 x y z &amp;V=-3 x y z^{2}+K_{3} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Comparing all three for uniqueness solution of potential<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c} K_{1}=K_{2}=0, \quad K_{3}=2 x y^{3} \\ \vec{E}=-\left(2 y^{3}-3 y z^{2}\right) \hat{x}-\left(6 x y^{2}-3 x z^{2}\right) \hat{y}+(6 x y z) \hat{z} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> as we know that [latex]E=-\nabla V [/latex]<br/>[latex] E=-\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x} \hat{x}+\frac{\partial}{\partial y} \hat{y}+\frac{\partial}{\partial z} \hat{z}\right) V \quad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> Clearly option (D) satisfies the given relation.</div>

The value of [latex]\psi _{B}-\psi _{A}[/latex] in Volts is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q53.jpg"/><noscript><img src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q53.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% QUESTION 93
% Number: Question 93
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Match column A with column B. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q25.jpg"/><noscript><img src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q25.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1\rightarrow P  \;  2\rightarrow Q  \; 3\rightarrow R[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1\rightarrow R  \; 2\rightarrow P  \; 3\rightarrow Q[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1\rightarrow Q  \;  2\rightarrow P  \; 3\rightarrow R[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1\rightarrow R  \; 2\rightarrow Q  \; 3\rightarrow P[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Point electromagnetic source radiates in all
direction
Dish antenna radiates any electromagnetic
energy in any particular direction with narrow
beam-width and high directivity
Yagi-uda antenna is high gain antenna used
for T.V. reception. Its radiation is along the axis
of the antenna.
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/a25.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/a25.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% QUESTION 94
% Number: Question 94
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For an antenna radiating in free space, the electric field at a distance of 1 km is found to be 12mV/m. Given that intrinsic impedance of the free space is [latex]120\pi \Omega[/latex], the magnitude of average power density due to this antenna at a distance of 2 km from the antenna (in nW/[latex]m^{2}[/latex]) is _____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given electric field, E at a distance of<br/>[latex] 1 \mathrm{km}=12 \mathrm{mV} / \mathrm{m}[/latex]<br/> Also we know that [latex]E \propto \frac{1}{r}[/latex], where r is the distance <br/> where electric field is to be measured. <br/> So, electric field, E at a distance of [latex]2 \mathrm{km} [/latex]<br/>[latex] =\frac{12 \mathrm{mV} / \mathrm{m}}{2}=6 \mathrm{mV} / \mathrm{m}[/latex]<br/> Also, power density due to the antenna is given as,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P_{\text {avg }} &amp;=\frac{1}{2} \frac{E^{2}}{\eta}=\frac{1}{2} \times \frac{6 \times 6 \times 10^{-6}}{120 \pi} \\ &amp;=47.7 \mathrm{nW} / \mathrm{m}^{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 97
% Number: Question 97
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-3, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In the following figure, the transmitter [latex]T_{X}[/latex]
sends a wideband modulated RF signal via a coaxial cable to the receiver [latex]R_{X}[/latex]. The output impedance [latex]Z_{T}[/latex] of [latex]T_{X}[/latex],
the characteristic impedance [latex]Z_{0}[/latex] of the cable and the input impedance [latex]Z_{R}[/latex] of [latex]R_{X}[/latex] are all real. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/q25.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/q25.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The signal gets distorted if [latex]Z_{R}\neq Z_{0}[/latex], irrespective of the value of [latex]Z_{T}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The signal gets distorted if [latex]Z_{T}\neq Z_{0}[/latex], irrespective of the value of [latex]Z_{R}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Signal distortion implies impedance mismatch at both ends :[latex]Z_{T}\neq Z_{0}[/latex] and [latex]Z_{R}\neq Z_{0}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Impedance mismatches do NOT result in signal distortion but reduce power
transfer efficiency</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The signal wave gets distorted if there is any
impedance mismatch between the characteristic
impedance [latex] Z_{0} [/latex] and the transmitting impedance [latex] Z_{T} [/latex]
and [latex] Z_{0} [/latex] and receiving impedance [latex] Z_{R} [/latex] <br/>
i.e. [latex] Z_{T} \neq Z_{0} [/latex] and [latex] Z_{R} \neq Z_{0} [/latex]<br/>
then only distortion occurs. <br/>
As standing waveforms the signal has multi
harmonics and hence distortion occurs.</div>

% QUESTION 98
% Number: Question 98
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-3, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider an air filled rectangular waveguide with a cross-section of 5cm x 3cm. For this waveguide, the cut-off frequency (in MHz) of [latex]TE_{21}[/latex] mode is ____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given [latex]a=5 \mathrm{cm}=5,10^{-2} \mathrm{m} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c} b=3 \mathrm{cm}=3 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{m} \\ f_{c}=\frac{C}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{n m}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{r m}{b}\right)^{2}} \\ f_{C T E_{21}}=\frac{C}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{2}{5 \mathrm{cm}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{3 \mathrm{cm}}\right)^{2}} \\ =\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{2 \times 100}{5}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{100}{3}\right)^{2}} \\ f_{c}=7810 \mathrm{MHz} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 99
% Number: Question 99
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For a rectangular waveguide of internal dimensions [latex]a \times b(a \gt b)[/latex], the cur-off
frequency for the [latex]TE_{11}[/latex] mode is the arithmetic mean of the cut-off frequencies for [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode and [latex]TE_{20}[/latex] mode. If a = [latex]\sqrt{5}[/latex] cm, the value of b (in cm) is______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Cut-off frequency of a rectangular waveguide is <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{c}&amp;=\frac{c}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{m \pi}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{n\pi}{b}\right)^{2}} \\ f_{c} \text{ for }\quad T E_{10}&amp;=\frac{c}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{1 \pi}{a}\right)^{2}}=\frac{c}{2 a} \\ f_{c} \text{ for }\quad T E_{20}&amp;=\frac{c}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{2 \pi}{a}\right)^{2}}=\frac{c}{2 a} \\ f_{c} \text { for } \quad T E_{11} &amp;=\frac{c}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{\pi}{a}\right)^{2}}+\left(\frac{\pi}{b}\right)^{2} \\ &amp;=\frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2}} \\ \text { Given, } f_{C T_{11}} &amp;=\frac{f_{C_{I E 10}}+f_{C I E_{20}}}{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \frac{c}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2}}&amp;=\frac{\frac{c}{2 a}+\frac{c}{a}}{2} \\ \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2}}&amp;=\frac{1}{2 a}+\frac{1}{a}=\frac{3}{2 a} \\ \left(\frac{1}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2}&amp;=\frac{9}{4 a^{2}} \\ \Rightarrow \quad\left(\frac{1}{b}\right)^{2} &amp;=\frac{5}{4 a^{2}} \\ b &amp;=\frac{2 a}{\sqrt{5}}=\frac{2 \times \sqrt{5}}{\sqrt{5}} \\ &amp;=2 \mathrm{cm} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

the ground is _____.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q54.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% QUESTION 101
% Number: Question 101
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">If the electric field of a plane wave is <br/> [latex]\vec{E}(z,t)=\hat{x}3 cos(\omega t-kz+30^{\circ})[/latex]- [latex]\hat{y}4sin(\omega t-kz+45^{\circ})[/latex] (mV/m), <br/> the polarization state of the plane wave is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">left elliptical</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">left circular</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">right elliptical</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">right circular</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E}(z, t)=3 \hat{x} \cos \left(\omega t-k z+30^{\circ}\right)-4 \hat{y} \sin \left(\omega t-k z+45^{\circ}\right)[/latex]<br/> The plane wave is travelling in +z direction<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \hat{E}_{x}=3 \cos \left(\omega t-k z+30^{\circ}\right) \\ \hat{E}_{y}=-4 \sin \left(\omega t-k z+45^{\circ}\right) \end{array}[/latex]<br/> At z=0, tracing E locus for various times<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c} \text { At } t=0, E_{x}=3 \cos 30^{\circ}=3 \cdot \frac{1}{2}=\frac{3}{2}=1.5 \\ E_{y}=-4 \sin 45^{\circ}=-2 \sqrt{2}=-2.83 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> At [latex]t=\frac{T}{6} [/latex]<br/>[latex] E_{x}=3 \cos \left(\frac{2 \pi}{T} \times \frac{T}{6}+30^{\circ}\right)=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] E_{y}=-4 \sin \left(60^{\circ}+45^{\circ}\right)=-3.86[/latex]<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a53.jpg"/></noscript><br/>This is left elliptical polarized plane-wave.</div>

% QUESTION 102
% Number: Question 102
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Which one of the following field patterns represents a TEM wave travelling in the
positive x direction ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]E=+8\hat{y}, H=-4\hat{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]E=-2\hat{y}, H=-3\hat{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]E=+2\hat{z}, H=+2\hat{y}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]E=-3\hat{y}, H=+4\hat{z}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Since, the TEM wave or plane wave is travelling in  positive x-direction, then conditions should be<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P &amp;=\vec{E} \times \vec{H} \\ \hat{a}_{x} &amp;=\hat{a}_{y} \times \hat{a}_{z}\\ \text{or, }\quad\hat{a}_{x}&amp;=\left(\hat{a}_{y}\right) \times\left(-\hat{a}_{z}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> So, the option (B) satisfies the condition.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 103
% Number: Question 103
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">To maximize power transfer, a lossless transmission line is to be matched to a resistive load impedance via a  [latex]\lambda/4[/latex]   transformer as shown. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q24.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q24.jpg"/></noscript><br/>  The characteristic impedance (in [latex]\Omega[/latex]) of the [latex]\lambda/4[/latex]  transformer is ___</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Input impedance for quarter wave transformer is given as,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Z_{\text {in }}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}^{2}}{Z_{L}} \\ \text { Given: } Z_{\text {in }}&amp;=50 \Omega, \quad Z_{L}=100 \Omega\\ \therefore \quad Z_{0}^{2}&amp;=50 \times 100 \\ Z_{0}&amp;=\sqrt{50 \times 100} \\ Z_{0}&amp;=70.72 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 104
% Number: Question 104
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The input impedance of a [latex]\frac{\lambda }{8}[/latex] section of a lossless transmission line of characteristic impedance 50 [latex]\Omega[/latex] is found to be real when the other end is terminated by a load [latex]Z_{L}=(R+jX)\Omega[/latex]. If X is  30 [latex]\Omega[/latex], the value of R(in [latex]\Omega[/latex]) is_____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} Z_{\text {in }}&amp;=Z_{0}\left(\frac{Z_{L}+j Z_{o} \tan \beta l}{Z_{o}+j Z_{L} \tan \beta l}\right)\\ \text{at }\;l&amp;=\frac{\lambda}{8} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \beta l&amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \times \frac{\lambda}{8}=\frac{\pi}{4} \\ \therefore \quad\tan \frac{\pi}{4}&amp;=1 \\ Z_{\text {in }}&amp;=Z_{o}\left[\frac{Z_{L}+j 50}{Z_{o}+j Z_{L}}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]
<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \text{Given},\quad Z_{L}&amp;=R+j X=R+j 30 \text{ and }Z_{0}=50 \Omega \\ 30 R &amp;=80(50+R) \\ Z_{\text {in }} &amp;=50\left[\frac{R+j 30+j 50}{50+j(R+j 30)}\right] \\ &amp;=50\left[\frac{R+j 80}{(50-30)+j R}\right]=50\left[\frac{R+j 80}{20+j R}\right] \\ &amp;=50\left[\frac{(R+j 80)(20-j R)}{20^{2}+R^{2}}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Since, only real part of [latex]Z_{\text {in }}[/latex] exists, so imaginary <br/> part of [latex]Z_{\text {in }}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \therefore \quad Z_{\text {in }} &amp;=50\left[\frac{20 R+1600 j-j R^{2}+50 R}{20^{2}+R^{2}}\right] \\ \text { Zimaginary } &amp;=0=\frac{1600-R^{2}}{20^{2}+R^{2}}\\ \therefore \quad R^{2} &amp;=1600 \\ R &amp;=40 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 105
% Number: Question 105
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For a parallel plate transmission line, let v be the speed of propagation and Z
be the characteristic impedance. Neglecting fringe effects, a reduction of the
spacing between the plates by a factor of two results in</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">halving of v and no change in Z</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">no changes in v and halving of Z</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">no change in both v and Z</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">halving of both v and Z</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} Z_{0}&amp;=\sqrt{\frac{L}{C}} \text{ for parallel plate line}\\ \frac{C}{l}&amp;=\frac{\epsilon W}{d} \text{ and } \frac{L}{l}=\frac{\mu d}{W} \\ \text{where}, \quad W&amp;= \text{Width of the line}\\ d &amp;=\text { Separation between the lines } \\ Z_{0} &amp; \propto d \quad (Z_{0}\text{ change by half})\\ \text { Velocity } &amp;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{L C}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0} \epsilon_{0}}}=3 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s}\\ &amp;\qquad\text{(No change in V)} \end{aligned}\\[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 106
% Number: Question 106
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">In spherical coordinates, let [latex]\hat{a}_{\theta },\hat{a}_{\phi }[/latex]
denote unit vectors along the [latex]\theta, \phi[/latex]  directions. <br/>[latex]E=\frac{100}{r}sin\theta cos(\omega t-\beta r)\hat{a}_\theta V/m[/latex] <br/> and <br/>  [latex]E=\frac{0.265}{r}sin\theta cos(\omega t-\beta r)\hat{a}_\phi A/m[/latex]  <br/>  represent the electric and magnetic field components of the EM wave at large
distances [latex]r[/latex]  from a dipole antenna, in free space. The average power (W) crossing
the hemispherical shell located at r = 1 km, [latex]0\leq \theta \leq \pi /2[/latex] is ____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \vec{E} &amp;=\operatorname{Re}\left[\frac{100}{r} \sin \theta e^{-\beta r} e^{j \omega t}\right] \hat{a}_{\theta} V / m \\ &amp;=\operatorname{Re}\left[\frac{0.265}{r} \sin \theta e^{-\beta \beta r} e^{j \omega t}\right] \hat{a}_{\phi} V / m \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Poynting vector,<br/>[latex] \vec{P}=\frac{1}{2} \operatorname{Re}\left[\vec{E} \times \vec{H}^{*}\right]=\frac{26.5}{2 r^{2}} \sin ^{2} \theta \hat{a}_{r} W / m^{2}[/latex]<br/> The average power crossing the given <br/> hemispherical shell is,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text { Power } &amp;=\int_{0}^{\pi / 2} \int_{\phi=0}^{2 \pi} \vec{P} \cdot r^{2} \sin \theta d \theta d \phi \hat{a}_{r} \\ &amp;=\frac{26.5}{2} \times 2 \pi \int_{\theta=0}^{\pi / 2} \sin ^{3} \theta d \theta \\ &amp;=\frac{26.5}{2} \times 2 \pi \frac{2}{3}=55.5 \mathrm{W} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 108
% Number: Question 108
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A two-port network has scattering parameters given by [latex][S]=\begin{bmatrix} S_{11} &amp;S_{12} \\ S_{21} &amp; S_{22} \end{bmatrix}[/latex].  If the
port-2 of the two port is short circuited, the [latex]S_{11}[/latex]
parameter for the resultant one port network is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{S_{11}-S_{11}S_{22}+S_{12}S_{21}}{1+S_{22}}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{S_{11}+S_{11}S_{22}-S_{12}S_{21}}{1+S_{22}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{S_{11}+S_{11}S_{22}+S_{12}S_{21}}{1-S_{22}}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{S_{11}-S_{11}S_{22}+S_{12}S_{21}}{1-S_{22}}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} b_{1}=s_{11} \cdot a_{1}+s_{12} \cdot a_{2} \qquad\ldots(i)\\ b_{2}=s_{21} \cdot a_{1}+s_{22} a_{2}\qquad\ldots(ii) \end{array}[/latex]<br/> When output is short, [latex]a_{2}=-b_{2} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} -a_{2} &amp;=s_{21} \cdot a_{1}+s_{22} a_{2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad a_{2} &amp;=\frac{-s_{21} \cdot a_{1}}{1+s_{22}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Substituting in first equation,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} b_{1} &amp;=s_{11} \cdot a_{1}+s_{12}\left(\frac{-s_{21} \cdot a_{1}}{1+s_{22}}\right) \\ &amp;=\frac{s_{11} \cdot a_{1}+s_{11} \cdot s_{22} a_{1}-s_{12} \cdot s_{21} a_{1}}{1+s_{22}} \\ \frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}} &amp;=s_{11}=\frac{s_{11}+s_{11} \cdot s_{22}-s_{12} \cdot s_{21}}{1+s_{22}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A monochromatic plane wave of wavelength [latex]\lambda =600\mu m[/latex] is propagating in the direction as shown in the figure below.[latex]\overrightarrow{E_{i}}[/latex] , [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{r}}[/latex] , and [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{t}}[/latex] denote incident, reflected, and transmitted electric field vectors associated with the wave <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q52.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q52.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The expression for [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{r}}[/latex] is</div>

% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-\frac{E_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\left ( \hat{a_{x}}+\hat{a_{z}} \right )e^{j\frac{\pi \times 10^{4}z}{3}}V/m[/latex]</div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">

% QUESTION 110
% Number: Question 110
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A monochromatic plane wave of wavelength [latex]\lambda =600\mu m[/latex] is propagating in the direction as shown in the figure below.[latex]\overrightarrow{E_{i}}[/latex] , [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{r}}[/latex] , and [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{t}}[/latex] denote incident, reflected, and transmitted electric field vectors associated with the wave <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q52.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q52.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The angle of incidence [latex]\theta _{i}[/latex] and the expression for [latex]\overrightarrow{E_{i}}[/latex] are</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]60^{\circ}[/latex] and [latex]\frac{E_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\left ( \hat{a_{x}}-\hat{a_{z}} \right )e^{-j\frac{\pi \times 10^{4}\left ( x+z \right )}{3\sqrt{2}}}V/m[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]45^{\circ}[/latex] and [latex]\frac{E_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\left ( \hat{a_{x}}+\hat{a_{z}} \right )e^{-j\frac{\pi \times 10^{4}z}{3}}V/m[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]45^{\circ}[/latex] and [latex]\frac{E_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\left ( \hat{a_{x}}-\hat{a_{z}} \right )e^{-j\frac{\pi \times 10^{4}\left ( x+z \right )}{3\sqrt{2}}}V/m[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]60^{\circ}[/latex] and [latex]\frac{E_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}\left ( \hat{a_{x}}-\hat{a_{z}} \right )e^{-j\frac{\pi \times 10^{4}z}{3}}V/m[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]E_{i} [/latex] propagates in ZXplane. <br/>[latex] E_{i} [/latex] directed in ZX-plane. <br/> It is a p-plane of incidence polarized wave.<br/> Using Snell's law,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{\sin \theta_{i}}{\sin 19.2^{\circ}} &amp;=\sqrt{\frac{4.5 \epsilon_{o}}{\epsilon_{o}}} &amp;\theta_{i}=45^{\circ} \\ \beta_{x} &amp;=\beta_{z}=\beta \cos 45^{\circ}=\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> (for propagation direction)<br/> For field direction [latex]=\frac{\hat{a}_{x}-\hat{a}_{z}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(45^{\circ}\right)[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 111
% Number: Question 111
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The return loss of a device is found to be 20 dB. The voltage standing wave ratio (VSWR) and magnitude of reflection coefficient are respectively</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1.22 and 0.1</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.81 and 0.1</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">- 1.22 and 0.1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.44 and 0.2</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { Return loss }&amp;=-20 \log |\Gamma| \\ -20 \log |\Gamma|&amp;=20 \\ \Rightarrow \quad|\Gamma|&amp;=10^{-1}=0.1 \\ \text{VSWR}&amp;=\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}=\frac{1+0.1}{1-0.1}=1.22 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

of the wire as shown below. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q53.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> The magnetic field inside the hole is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">uniform and depends only on d</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">uniform and depends only on b</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">uniform and depends on both b and d</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">non uniform</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/a53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/a53.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The H field due to entire cylinder without hole.<br/>[latex] H\left(r_{1}\right)=\frac{J r_{1}}{2}[/latex]<br/> The Hfield due to hole only.<br/>[latex] H\left(r_{2}\right)=\frac{J r_{2}}{2}[/latex]<br/> Current inside the hole is zero means<br/>[latex] H(r)=\frac{J r_{1}}{2}-\frac{J r_{2}}{2} \simeq \frac{J d}{2}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 113
% Number: Question 113
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">An infinitely long uniform solid wire of radius a carries a uniform dc current of density [latex]\overrightarrow{j}[/latex]. <br/><br/>
The magnetic field at a distance [latex]r[/latex]  from the center of the wire is proportional to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]r \; for \; r \lt a \; and \; 1/r^{2}\; for \;r \gt a[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex] 0 \; for \; r  \lt  a \; and \; 1 /r \; for \; r  \gt  a [/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex] r \; for \; r  \lt  a \; and \; 1 /r \; for \; r  \gt  a [/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0 \; for\; r \lt a \; and \; 1/r^{2} \;for \; r \gt a[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
We know that magnetic flux density at a distance r from the wire is, <br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} |B| &amp;=\frac{\mu_{0} I}{2 \pi r} \\ \text { For } r \lt a \rightarrow I&amp;=J \cdot \pi r^{2} \\ \text { so } \quad |B|&amp;=\frac{\mu_{0} J r}{2}\\ |B| \propto r \text { for } r \lt a \\ \text { for } r \gt a,\quad I&amp;=J \times \pi a^{2} \\ \text { So, } |B|&amp;=\frac{\mu_{0} J \pi a^{2}}{2 \pi r} \\ |B| \propto \frac{1}{r} \text { for } r \gt a \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q40.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q40.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> The phase velocity [latex]v_{p}[/latex] of the wave inside the waveguide satisfies</div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">

% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A transmission line with a characteristic impedance of 100 [latex]\Omega[/latex] is used to match a 50 [latex]\Omega[/latex] section to a 200 [latex]\Omega[/latex] section. If the matching is to be done both at 429 MHz and 1 GHz, the length of the transmission line can be approximately</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.75 cm</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The [latex] 100 \Omega [/latex] device used to match [latex] 50 \Omega [/latex] and [latex] 200 \Omega [/latex] devices is a quarter wave transformer. <br/>Length of matching device<br/>
[latex]
=(2 n+1) \frac{\lambda}{4}=\text { odd multiple }
[/latex]<br/>
Where [latex] n [/latex] -depends on frequency of [latex] \lambda / 4 [/latex]<br/>
[latex]
\lambda_{1} \text { of frequency } 429 \mathrm{MHz}=0.7 \mathrm{m}
[/latex]<br/>
[latex] \lambda_{2} [/latex] of frequency [latex] 1 \mathrm{GHz}=0.3 \mathrm{m} [/latex]<br/>
Common multiple [latex] =2.1 \mathrm{m} [/latex]<br/>
Dividing by [latex] 4=0.525 \mathrm{m} [/latex] (not available in options) <br/>Another odd multiple of [latex] 0.525 \times 3=1.58 \mathrm{m} [/latex]</div>
