% QUESTION 116
% Number: Question 116
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The radiation pattern of an antenna in spherical co-ordinates is given by <br/> [latex]F(\theta )=cos^{4}\theta ;0\leq \theta\leq \pi /2[/latex] <br/>The directivity of the antenna is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">10 dB</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">12.6 dB</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">11.5 dB</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">18 dB</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]F(\theta)=\cos ^{4} \theta, 0 \leq \theta \leq \pi / 2[/latex]<br/> we know that directivity D is<br/>[latex] D=\frac{4 \pi u_{\max }}{\pi_{\mathrm{rad}}} \qquad\ldots(1)[/latex]<br/>[latex] \mathrm{F}(\theta) [/latex] is nothing but radiation intensity [latex] u(\theta, \phi)[/latex] and<br/>[latex] W_{\mathrm{rad}} [/latex] is radiated power.<br/>[latex] W_{\mathrm{rad}}=\int_{0}^{2 \pi \pi / 2} \int_{0}^{2} \cos ^{4} \theta \sin \theta d \phi\qquad\ldots(2)[/latex]<br/> above equation is written from the formula<br/>[latex] W_{\mathrm{rad}}=\iint_{\theta \phi} u(\theta, \phi) d \Omega[/latex]<br/> where [latex]\alpha \Omega[/latex] is solid angle and [latex]d \Omega=\sin \theta d \theta d \phi [/latex]<br/> So from (2)<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} W_{\mathrm{rad}} &amp;=\int_{0}^{2 \pi \pi / 2} \int_{0}^{2} \cos ^{4} \theta \sin d \theta d \phi \\ W_{\mathrm{rad}} &amp;=2 \pi \int_{0}^{\pi / 2} \cos ^{4} \theta \sin \theta d \theta \\W_{\mathrm{rad}}&amp;=-2 \pi\left[\frac{\cos ^{5} \theta}{5}\right]_{0}^{\pi / 2}\\
W_{\mathrm{rad}}&amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{5}&amp;\ldots(3)\\
\text { So, } \quad D&amp;=\frac{4 \pi u_{\max }}{\left(\frac{2 \pi}{5}\right)}=10 u_{\max }=10\\
&amp;\text { In } \mathrm{dB} \text { directivity }=10 \mathrm{log}_{10} \mathrm{D}=10 \mathrm{dB}\end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A coaxial cable with an inner diameter of 1 mm and outer diameter of 2.4 mm is filled with a
dielectric of relative permittivity 10.89. Given [latex]\mu _{0}=4 \pi \times 10^{-7} H/m,[/latex] [latex]\varepsilon _{0}=\frac{10^{-9}}{36 \pi} F/m[/latex], the characteristic impedance of the cable is</div>

% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">100 [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>

% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">15.3 [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Characteristics impedance of the co-axial cable is given by <br/>[latex] Z_{o}=\frac{138}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}} \log \frac{D}{d}[/latex]<br/>[latex] =\frac{138}{\sqrt{10.89}} \log \left(\frac{2.4}{1}\right)=15.89 \Omega[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The electric and magnetic fields for a TEM wave of frequency 14 GHz in a
homogeneous medium of relative permittivity er and relative permeability [latex]\mu _{r}[/latex]=1 are given by<br/> [latex]\vec{E}=E_{p}e^{j(\omega t-280\pi t)}\hat{u}_{z} V/m [/latex] <br/>[latex] \vec{H}=3e^{j(\omega t-280\pi y)}\hat{u}_{x} V/m[/latex] <br/><br/>Assuming the speed of light in free space to be [latex]3 \times 10^{8}[/latex] m/s, the intrinsic impedance of free space to be 120[latex]\pi[/latex], the relative permittivity [latex]\varepsilon_{r}[/latex] of the medium and the electric field amplitude [latex]E_{p}[/latex] are</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\varepsilon _{r}=3,E_{p}=120 \pi[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\varepsilon _{r}=3,E_{p}=360 \pi[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\varepsilon _{r}=9,E_{p}=360 \pi[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\varepsilon _{r}=9,E_{p}=120 \pi[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text{Given:}\quad\vec{E}&amp;=E_{p} e^{i(\omega t-280 \pi y)} \hat{u}_{z} V / m\\ \vec{H}&amp;=3 e^{i(\omega t-280 \pi y)} \hat{u}_{x} A / m \\ v&amp;=3 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> From given expression we conclude that,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \beta &amp;=280 \pi=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \\ \therefore \quad &amp; \lambda=\frac{1}{140} \text { meter } \\ v &amp;=f \lambda=14 \times 10^{9} \times \frac{1}{140} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{se} \\ v &amp;=1 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{sec} \\ \text { but, } \quad v &amp;=\frac{c}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r} \mu_{r}}} \\ \therefore \quad 1 \times 10^{8} &amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{\sqrt{1 \times \epsilon_{r}}} \\ \therefore \quad \epsilon_{r}&amp;=9\\ \frac{E_{P}}{H_{P}}&amp;=\eta=\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{\epsilon}}=\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0} \times 1}{\epsilon_{0} \times 9}}=\frac{1}{3} \sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}} \\ \Rightarrow \frac{E_{P}}{3}&amp;=\frac{1}{3} \times 120 \pi \\ \therefore \quad E_{P}&amp;=120 \pi \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 121
% Number: Question 121
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A transmission line of characteristic impedance 50[latex]\Omega[/latex] is terminated in a load
impedance [latex]Z_{L}[/latex]. The VSWR of the line is measured as 5 and the first of the
voltage maxima in the line is observed at a distance of [latex]\frac{\lambda }{4}[/latex] from the load. The value of [latex]Z_{L}[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">10 [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">250 [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(19.23 + j46.15) [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(19.23 - j 46.15) [latex]\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\text { VSWR }=5=\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore |\Gamma|=\frac{2}{3} \Rightarrow \Gamma=\pm \frac{2}{3}[/latex]<br/> If maximum is at [latex] \lambda / 4 [/latex] from load then minimum will be at load itself.<br/> For the phase of [latex]\Gamma [/latex]<br/>[latex] 2 \beta x_{\max }=2 n \pi+\theta[/latex]<br/> where [latex]x_{\max }[/latex] is distance of [latex]V_{\max }[/latex] from load.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 2 \cdot \frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{\lambda}{4}&amp;=\theta \\ \Gamma&amp;=-\frac{2}{3}\\ \text{Now},-\frac{2}{3}&amp;=\frac{Z_{L}-Z_{0}}{Z_{L}+Z_{0}}=\frac{Z_{L}-50}{Z_{L}+50}\\ \Rightarrow Z_{L}&amp;=10 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 122
% Number: Question 122
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A current sheet [latex]\bar{J}[/latex] = 10[latex]\hat{u}_{y}[/latex] A/m lies on the dielectric interface [latex]x=0[/latex] between two dielectric media with [latex]\varepsilon _{r1}[/latex]=5, [latex]\mu _{r1}[/latex]=1 in Region-1 [latex] (x \lt 0) [/latex] and [latex]\varepsilon _{r2}[/latex]=5, [latex]\mu _{r2}[/latex]=2 in Region-2 [latex] (x \gt 0)[/latex]. If the magnetic field in Region-1 at [latex]x = 0^{-}[/latex] is [latex]\vec{H}_{1} = 3\hat{u}_{x}[/latex] + 30[latex]\hat{u}_{y}[/latex] A/m. The magnetic field in Region-2 at [latex]x = 0^{+}[/latex] is  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q26.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q26.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{H_{2}}=1.5\hat{u}_{x}+30\hat{u}_{y}-10\hat{u}_{z} A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{H}_{2}=3\hat{u}_{x}+30\hat{u}_{y}-10\hat{u}_{z} A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{H}_{2}=1.5\hat{u}_{x}+40\hat{u}_{y} A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{H}_{2}=3\hat{u}_{x}+30\hat{u}_{y}+10\hat{u}_{z} A/m[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For magnetic field boundary relations are <br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \vec{B}_{n_{1}} &amp;=\vec{B}_{n_{2}} \\ \text { and } \vec{H}_{t_{1}}-\vec{H}_{t_{2}} &amp;=-\vec{J}_{s} \times \vec{a}_{n} \\ \Rightarrow \quad B_{x_{1}}&amp;=B_{x_{2}}\qquad\text{(x is normal component)}\\ \mu_{1} H_{x_{1}} &amp;=\mu_{2} H_{x 2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad 1 \times 3 &amp;=2 \times H_{x 2} \\ H_{x_{2}} &amp;=1.5 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \vec{H}_{11}-\vec{H}_{t_{2}} &amp;=-10 \hat{u}_{y} \times \hat{u}_{x}=+10 \hat{u}_{z} \\ \vec{H}_{t_{2}} &amp;=H_{t_{1}}-10 \hat{u}_{z} \\ \vec{H}_{t_{2}} &amp;=30 \hat{u}_{y}-10 \hat{u}_{z} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \vec{H}_{2} &amp;=1.5 \hat{u}_{x}+30 \hat{u}_{y}-10 \hat{u}_{z} \mathrm{A} / \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The modes in a rectangular waveguide are denoted by [latex]\frac{TE_{mn}}{TM_{mn}}[/latex]  where
m and n are the eigen numbers along the larger and smaller dimensions of the
waveguide respectively. Which one of the following statements is TRUE?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">The [latex]TM_{10}[/latex] mode of the wave does not exist</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode of the wave does not exist</div>

In case of rectangular wave guide  [latex]T E_{\text {rm }}[/latex]  exists for  all values of  m  and  n except  m=0  and  n=0 . For   [latex]T M_{m n}[/latex]  Io exist both values of mand  n  must be non
zero</div>

% QUESTION 124
% Number: Question 124
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A transmission line of characteristic impedance 50 W is terminated by a 50 [latex]\Omega[/latex] load. When excited by a sinusoidal voltage source at 10 GHz, the phase
difference between two points spaced 2 mm apart on the line is found to [latex]\frac{\pi }{4}[/latex] radians. The phase velocity of the wave along the line is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.8 \times 10^{8} m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.2 \times 10^{8} m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.6 \times 10^{8} m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3 \times 10^{8} m/s[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { Phase difference }&amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \text { (path difference) } \\ \Rightarrow \frac{\pi}{4}&amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda}\left(2 \times 10^{-3}\right) \\ \therefore \quad &amp;=8 \times 2 \times 10^{-3} \\ &amp;=16 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{m} \\ f&amp;= 10 \mathrm{GHz}=10 \times 10^{9} \mathrm{Hz} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Hence the phase velocity of wave along the line is,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} &amp; v=f \lambda=10 \times 10^{9} \times 16 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{sec} \\ \therefore \quad v &amp;=1.6 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 125
% Number: Question 125
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the following statements regarding the complex Poynting vector [latex]\vec{P}[/latex] for the power radiated by a point source in an infinite homogeneous and lossless medium. Re([latex]\vec{P}[/latex]) denotes the real part of [latex]\vec{P}[/latex], S denotes a spherical surface whose centre is at the point source, and [latex]\hat{n}[/latex]
denotes the unit surface normal on S. Which of the following statements is TRUE?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Re([latex]\vec{P}[/latex]) remains constant at any radial distance from the source</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Re([latex]\vec{P}[/latex]) increases with increasing radial distance from the source</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint \oint_{S}Re(\vec{P}).\hat{n} dS[/latex] remains constant at any radial distance from the source</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint \oint_{S}Re(\vec{P}).\hat{n} dS[/latex] decreases with increasing radial distance form the source</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Power density of any point source decreases with
distance i.e. the density decreases and area of
cross-over increases with the product being
constant.</div>

% QUESTION 126
% Number: Question 126
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In the circuit shown, all the transmission line sections are lossless. The Voltage
Standing Wave Ration(VSWR) on the 60 [latex]\Omega[/latex] line is  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q47.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.00</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1.64</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.50</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3.00</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]Z_{\text {in }}=Z_{0}\left(\frac{Z_{L}+j Z_{0} \tan \beta l}{Z_{0}+j Z_{L} \tan \beta l}\right)[/latex]<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/a47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/a47.jpg"/></noscript><br/> Input impedance,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Z_{1} &amp;=30\left[\frac{0+j 30 \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda}\right)\left(\frac{\lambda}{8}\right)}{30+0}\right] \\ \Rightarrow Z_{1} &amp;=j 30 \\ Z_{2} &amp;=30 \sqrt{2}\left[\frac{30+j 30 \sqrt{2} \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda}\right)\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)}{30 \sqrt{2}+j 30 \tan \left(\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda}\right)\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)}\right] \\ &amp;=30 \sqrt{2}\left[\frac{\frac{30}{\tan \pi / 2}+j 30 \sqrt{2}}{\frac{30 \sqrt{2}}{\tan \pi / 2}+j 30}\right] \\ &amp;=60 \Omega \\ \text { or, } Z_{2}&amp;=\frac{(30 \sqrt{2})^{2}}{30}=60 \Omega &amp; \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Load impedance,<br/>[latex] Z_{L}=Z_{1}+Z_{2}=j 30+6[/latex]<br/> Magnitude of reflection coefficient,<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} |\Gamma|&amp;=\left|\frac{Z_{L}-Z_{0}}{Z_{L}+Z_{0}}\right|=\left|\frac{60+j 30-60}{60+j 30+60}\right|\\ &amp;=\left|\frac{j 30}{120+j 30}\right|=\mid \frac{j 1}{4+j \mid} \\ &amp;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{16+1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{17}}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> VSWR on 60 \Omega line,<br/>[latex] \text { VSWR }=\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}=\frac{1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{17}}}{1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{17}}}=1.64[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 127
% Number: Question 127
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A plane wave having the electric field components [latex]\vec{E}_{i}=24 \cos (3 \times 10^{8}t-\beta y)\hat{a}_{z} V/m[/latex] and traveling in free space is incident normally on a lossless medium with [latex]\mu =\mu_{0} [/latex] and [latex] \varepsilon =9\varepsilon_{0}[/latex] which occupies the region [latex]y\geq 0[/latex]. The reflected magnetic field component is given by</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{10\pi }cos(3 \times 10^{8}t+y)\hat{a}_{x} \; A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{20\pi }cos(3 \times 10^{8}t+y)\hat{a}_{x}\; A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-\frac{1}{20\pi }cos(3 \times 10^{8}t+y)\hat{a}_{x} \; A/m[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-\frac{1}{10\pi }cos(3 \times 10^{8}t+y)\hat{a}_{x} \; A/m[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\vec{E}_{i}=24 \cos \left(3 \times 10^{8} t-\beta y\right) \hat{a}_{z} \vee / m[/latex]<br/> The wave is travelling in +y direction.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} H_{i}=&amp; \frac{1}{\eta}\left(\hat{a}_{y} \times \vec{E}_{i}\right) \\ =&amp; \frac{24 \cos }{120 \pi}\left(3 \times 10^{8} t-\beta y\right) \hat{a}_{x} \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{5 \pi} \cos \left(3 \times 10^{8} t-\beta y\right) \hat{a}_{x} \\ \frac{H_{r}}{H_{i}}&amp;=\frac{\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}}{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}}=\frac{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{1}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{2}}}}{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{2}}}} \\ =\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}-\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}}&amp;=\frac{\sqrt{9}-\sqrt{1}}{\sqrt{9}+\sqrt{1}}=\frac{3-1}{3+1}=\frac{2}{4}=\frac{1}{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Therefore, reflected magnetic field component,<br/>[latex]\vec{H}_{r}=\frac{1}{10 \pi} \cos \left(3 \times 10^{8} t+\beta y\right) \hat{a}_{x}[/latex]<br/>Due to change in propagation direction to -y</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 128
% Number: Question 128
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The electric field component of a time harmonic plane EM wave traveling in
a nonmagnetic lossless dielectric medium has an amplitude of 1 V/m. If the
relative permittivity of the medium is 4, the magnitude of the time-average
power density vector (in W/[latex]m^{2}[/latex]) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{30\pi }[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{60\pi }[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{120\pi }[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{240\pi }[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Magnitude of the time-average power density<br/> vector<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P_{a v} &amp;=\frac{E^{2}}{2 \eta} \\ \text { where, } \quad\eta &amp;=\sqrt{\mu / \epsilon} \\ \eta &amp;=\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{4 \epsilon_{0}}}=\frac{\eta_{0}}{2}=\frac{120 \pi}{2} \\ P_{a v} &amp;=\frac{1^{2}}{2\left(\frac{120 \pi}{2}\right)}=\frac{1}{120 \pi} \mathrm{W} / \mathrm{m}^{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 129
% Number: Question 129
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A transmission line has a characteristic impedance of 50 [latex]\Omega[/latex] and a resistance of 0.1[latex]\Omega[/latex]/m . If the line is distortion less, the attenuation constant(in Np/m) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">500</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.014</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.002</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For distortionless transmission line,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} L G&amp;=R C \\ \Rightarrow \frac{L}{C}&amp;=\frac{R}{G} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Characteristic impedance,<br/>[latex] Z_{0}=\sqrt{\frac{L}{C}}=\sqrt{\frac{R}{G}}[/latex]<br/> Attenuation constant,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \alpha &amp;=\sqrt{R G}=\sqrt{R} \cdot \frac{\sqrt{R}}{Z_{0}}=\frac{R}{Z_{0}}=\frac{0.1}{50} \\ &amp;=0.002 \mathrm{Np} / \mathrm{m} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 130
% Number: Question 130
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Miscellaneous Topics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If the scattering matrix [S] of a two port network is <br/>[latex]|S|=\begin{bmatrix} 0.2\angle 0^{\circ}&amp;0.9\angle 90^{\circ} \\ 0.9\angle 90^{\circ}&amp; 0.1\angle 90^{\circ} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]<br/> then the network is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">lossless and reciprocal</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">lossless but not reciprocal</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">not lossless but reciprocal</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">neither lossless nor reciprocal</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For reciprocal networks, [latex] S_{12}=S_{21} [/latex]<br/> For symmetrical networks, [latex] S_{11}=S_{22} [/latex]<br/> For antimetrical networks, [latex] S_{11}=-S_{22} [/latex]<br/> For lossless reciprocal networks, <br/>[latex] \left|S_{11}\right|=\left|S_{22}\right| [/latex]<br/>
and [latex] \left|S_{11}\right|^{2}+\left|S_{12}\right|^{2}=1 [/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 131
% Number: Question 131
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A transmission line terminates in two branches, each of length [latex]\frac{\lambda }{4}[/latex], as shown. The branches are terminated by 50[latex]\Omega[/latex] loads. The lines are lossless and have the characteristic impedances shown. Determine the impedance [latex]Z_{i}[/latex] as seen by the source <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q50.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q50.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]200\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]100\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]50\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]25\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} z_{1}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}^{2}}{Z_{4}}=\frac{(100)^{2}}{50}=200 \Omega \\ Z_{2}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}^{2}}{Z_{2}}=\frac{(100)^{2}}{50}=200 \Omega \\ Z_{L}&amp;=Z_{1} \| I_{2}=100 \Omega \\ Z_{i}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}^{2}}{Z_{L}}=\frac{(50)^{2}}{100}=25 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 132
% Number: Question 132
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A magnetic field in air is measured to be <br/> [latex]\vec{B}=B_{0}(\frac{x}{x^{2}+y^{2}}\hat{y}-\frac{y}{x^{2}+y^{2}}\hat{x})[/latex] <br/>What current distribution leads to this field ?
[Hint : The algebra is trivial in cylindrical coordinates.]</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{J}=\frac{B_{0}\hat{z}}{\mu _{0}}(\frac{1}{x^{2}+y^{2}}),r\neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{J}=\frac{B_{0}\hat{z}}{\mu _{0}}(\frac{2}{x^{2}+y^{2}}),r\neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{J}=0,r\neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\vec{J}=\frac{B_{0}\hat{z}}{\mu _{0}}(\frac{1}{x^{2}+y^{2}}),r\neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Applying [latex]\nabla \cdot H=J [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \nabla \times B&amp;=\left|\begin{array}{ccc} \hat{a}_{x} &amp; \hat{a}_{y} &amp; \hat{a}_{z} \\ \frac{\partial}{\partial x} &amp; \frac{\partial}{\partial y} &amp; \frac{\partial}{\partial z} \\ \frac{-y}{x^{2}+y^{2}} &amp; \frac{x}{x^{2}+y^{2}} &amp; 0 \end{array}\right|=0 \\ J&amp;=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 133
% Number: Question 133
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If a vector field [latex]\vec{V}[/latex] is related to another vector field [latex]\vec{A}[/latex] through [latex]\vec{V}=\bigtriangledown *\vec{A}[/latex], which of the following is true? (Note : C and [latex]S_C[/latex] refer to any closed contour and any surface whose boundary is C.)</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint_{C}^{}\vec{V} \cdot \vec{dl}=\int_{S_{C}}^{} \int_{}^{}\vec{A}\cdot \vec{dS}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint_{C}^{}\vec{A} \cdot \vec{dl}=\int_{S_{C}}^{} \int_{}^{}\vec{V}\cdot \vec{dS}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint_{C}^{}\triangledown \times \vec{V} \cdot \vec{dl}=\int_{S_{C}}^{} \int_{}^{}\triangledown \times \vec{A}\cdot \vec{dS}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\oint_{C}^{}\triangledown \times \vec{A} \cdot \vec{dl}=\int_{S_{C}}^{} \int_{}^{}\vec{V}\cdot \vec{dS}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text"></div>

Which components of the resulting magnetic field are non-zero at the origin ? <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q20.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q20.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% QUESTION 136
% Number: Question 136
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">At 20 GHz, the gain of a parabolic dish antenna of 1 meter and 70% efficiency is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">15 dB</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">25 dB</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">35 dB</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">45 dB</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\text { Gain }=\frac{4 \pi}{\lambda^{2}} A_{e}=\frac{4 \pi}{\lambda^{2}} \pi R^{2}[/latex]<br/> Along with illumination or efficiency<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text { Gain } &amp;=\eta \pi^{2} \frac{(2 R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}=7\left(\frac{D}{\lambda}\right)^{2} \\ &amp;=0.7 \pi^{2}\left(\frac{1 \times 20 \times 10^{9}}{3 \times 10^{8}}\right)^{2} \\ &amp;=30705.4359 \\ G(\mathrm{dB}) &amp;=10 \log _{10} G \\ &amp;=10 \log _{10}(30705.4359)=45 \mathrm{dB} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 138
% Number: Question 138
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A uniform plane wave in the free space is normally incident on an infinitely
thick dielectric slab (dielectric constant [latex]\varepsilon_{r}[/latex]=9). The magnitude of the reflection coefficient is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.3</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.5</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.8</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Dielectric constant of free space, [latex]\epsilon_{r_{1}}=1 [/latex]<br/> Dielectric constant of dielectric slab, [latex]\epsilon_{r_{2}}=9 [/latex]<br/> Magnitude of reflection coefficient <br/>[latex] |\Gamma|=\left|\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{1}}}-\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{2}}}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{1}}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{r_{2}}}}\right|=\left|\frac{1-3}{1+3}\right|=\frac{2}{4}=0.5[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 139
% Number: Question 139
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">One end of a loss-less transmission line having the characteristic impedance of
75[latex]\Omega[/latex] and length of 1 cm is short-circuited. At 3 GHz, the input impedance at the other end of transmission line is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Resistive</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Capacitive</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Inductive</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
It is a short circuit line with [latex]l =1 cm.[/latex]<br/> As [latex]\quad \lambda=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{3 \times 10^{9}}=0.3=30 \mathrm{cm} [/latex]<br/>[latex] l \lt \frac{\lambda}{4}[/latex]<br/> The line has inductive nature.<br/> As [latex]Z_{S C}=j Z_{o} \tan \beta l[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 140
% Number: Question 140
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A rectangular waveguide of internal dimensions (a=4cm and b=3cm) is to
be operated in [latex]TE_{11}[/latex] mode. The minimum operating frequency is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">6.25 GHz</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">6.0 GHz</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5.0 GHz</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3.75 GHz</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Minimum operating frequency,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} f_{0}&amp;=\frac{c}{2 \pi} \sqrt{\left(\frac{m \pi}{a}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{n \pi}{b}\right)^{2}}\\ \text{For }\;T E_{11}&amp;\;\text{ mode}\\ f_{0} &amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2} \sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{0.04}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{0.03}\right)^{2}} \\ &amp;=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2} \times \frac{0.05}{0.04 \times 0.03}=6.25 \mathrm{GHz} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 141
% Number: Question 141
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For static electric and magnetic fields in an inhomogeneous source-free medium, which of the following represents the correct form of Maxwell's equations ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\bigtriangledown \cdot E=0,\bigtriangledown \times B=0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\bigtriangledown \cdot E=0,\bigtriangledown \cdot B=0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\bigtriangledown  \times  E=0,\bigtriangledown  \times B=0[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\bigtriangledown  \times  E=0,\bigtriangledown \cdot B=0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Intensity - curl - line integral <br/> Density divergence - surface integral<br/> This is order of understanding Maxwell's equations. <br/> For any material<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{cc} \nabla \times E=0 &amp; \nabla \cdot B=0 \\ \nabla \times H=J &amp; \nabla \cdot D=\rho_{v} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For source free like charge or current free conditions<br/>[latex] \nabla \times E=0 \quad \nabla \cdot B=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] \nabla \times H=0 \quad \nabla \cdot D=0[/latex]<br/> If [latex]D=\epsilon[/latex] E and [latex]B=\mu[/latex] H and [latex]\in[/latex] or [latex]\mu[/latex] are constant with  distance or direction then<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \nabla \cdot(\in E) &amp;=0 &amp; &amp; \nabla \times \frac{B}{\mu}=0 \\ \nabla \cdot E &amp;=0 &amp; &amp; \nabla \times B=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> These are called as homogeneous/isotropic medium. <br/> For non-homogeneous material. However they are not valid.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 142
% Number: Question 142
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For a Hertz dipole antenna, the half power beam width (HPBW) in the E-plane is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]360^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]180^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]90^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]45^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
E-plane mean [latex]\theta[/latex]-plane or vertical plane <br/>[latex] E \propto \sin \theta [/latex] in Hertzian dipole<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} E_{\max } \text { when } \theta=90^{\circ} \\ E_{\min } \text { when } \theta=0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> E has half power of maximum when [latex]\theta=45^{\circ} \; and\;  180^{\circ}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \text { HPBW in } \theta \text { side }=135^{\circ}-45^{\circ}=90^{\circ}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 143
% Number: Question 143
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1"> A right circularly polarized (RCP) plane wave is incident at an angle [latex]60^{\circ}[/latex] to the
normal, on an air-dielectric interface. If the reflected wave is linearly polarized,
the relative dielectric constant [latex]\varepsilon_{r2}[/latex]
is.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q70.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q70.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{3}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The reflected wave is linearly polarized when circularly polarized wave is incident under Brewster's angle of incidence.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \tan \theta_{B} &amp;=\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon_{r_{2}}}{\epsilon_{r_{1}}}}=\tan 60^{\circ} \\ \epsilon_{r_{2}} &amp;=3 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Number: Question 145
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1"> The parallel branches of a 2-wire transmission line re terminated in 100[latex]\Omega[/latex] and
200[latex]\Omega[/latex] resistors as shown in the figure. The characteristic impedance of the line is [latex]Z_{0} =  50\Omega[/latex] and each section has a length of [latex]\frac{\lambda }{4}[/latex]. The voltage reflection coefficient [latex]\Gamma[/latex] at the input is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q68.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q68.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-j\frac{7}{5}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-5}{7}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]j \frac{5}{7}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{7}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/a68.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/a68.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} Z_{\text {in }}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}^{2}}{Z_{L}} ; if l=\frac{\lambda}{4} \\ R_{1} \text{ due to }100 \Omega&amp;=\frac{50^{2}}{100}=25 \\ R_{2} \text{ due to }200 \Omega&amp;=\frac{50^{2}}{200}=\frac{25}{2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad R_{1} \| R_{2} &amp;=25 \text { II } \frac{25}{2}=\frac{25}{3} \\ Z_{S} &amp;=\frac{(50)^{2}}{25 / 3}=300 \Omega \\ \therefore \quad\Gamma&amp;=\frac{Z_{S}-Z_{0}}{Z_{S}+Z_{0}}=\frac{300-50}{300+50}=\frac{5}{7} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The [latex]\vec{E}[/latex] field in a rectangular waveguide of inner dimension [latex]a \times b[/latex]
is given by <br/>[latex]\vec{E}=\frac{\omega \mu }{h^{2}}(\frac{\pi}{a})H_{0}sin(\frac{2\pi x}{a})^{2}sin(\omega t-\beta z)\hat{y}[/latex] <br/>where  [latex]H_{0}[/latex] is a constant, and a and b are the dimensions along the x-axis and the y-axis respectively. The mode of propagation in the waveguide is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]TE_{20}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]TM_{11}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 148
% Number: Question 148
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The [latex]\vec{H}[/latex] field (in A/m) of a plane wave propagating in free space is given by <br/> [latex]\vec{H}=\hat{x}\frac{5\sqrt{3}}{\eta _{0}}cos(\omega t-\beta z)+\hat{y}\frac{5}{\eta _{0}}sin(\omega t-\beta z+\frac{\pi }{2})[/latex].  <br/>The time average power flow density in Watts is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\eta _{0}}{100}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{100}{\eta _{0}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]50 {\eta _{0}}^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{50}{\eta _{0}}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For free space,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P&amp;=\frac{E^{2}}{2 \eta_{0}}, E=\eta_{0} H \\ P&amp;=\frac{\eta_{0}^{2} H^{2}}{2 \eta_{0}}=\frac{\eta_{0} H^{2}}{2} \\ H&amp;=\frac{1}{\eta_{0}} \sqrt{(5 \sqrt{3})^{2}+(5)^{2}}=\frac{10}{\eta_{0}} \\ P&amp;=\frac{\eta_{0}}{2} \times \frac{100}{\eta_{0}^{2}}=\frac{50}{\eta_{0}} \text { watts } \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 149
% Number: Question 149
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">An air-filled rectangular waveguide has inner dimensions of 3cm x 2cm.
The wave impedance of the TE20  mode of propagation in the waveguide at a
frequency of 30 GHz is (free space impedance [latex]\eta_{0}[/latex] = 377 [latex]\Omega[/latex])</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]308\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]355\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]400\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]461\Omega[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { For } TE_{20}, \quad \lambda_{C}&amp;=a=3 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{m} \\ f_{C}&amp;= \frac{C}{\lambda_{C}}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{3 \times 10^{-2}}=10^{10} \mathrm{Hz} \\ \eta_{T E}&amp;=\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{f_{c}}{f}\right)^{2}}} =\frac{377}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{10^{10}}{3 \times 10^{10}}\right)^{2}}}\\ \eta_{T E}&amp;=400 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 152
% Number: Question 152
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A 30-Volts battery with zero source resistance is connected to a coaxial line of
characteristic impedance of 50 Ohms at t=0 second terminated in an unknown resistive load. The line length is that it takes 400 [latex]\mu[/latex]s for an electromagnetic wave to travel from source end to load end and vice-versa. At  t=400 [latex]\mu[/latex]s,  the voltage at the load end is
found to be 40 Volts. <br/><br/> The steady-state current through the load resistance is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.2 Amps</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.3 Amps</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.6 Amps</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.4 Amps</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
In steady state, [latex] V_{L}=30 \mathrm{V} [/latex]<br/>[latex] I_{L}=\frac{30}{100}=0.3 \mathrm{A}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 153
% Number: Question 153
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A 30-Volts battery with zero source resistance is connected to a coaxial line of
characteristic impedance of 50 Ohms at t=0 second terminated in an unknown resistive load. The line length is that it takes 400 [latex]\mu[/latex]s for an electromagnetic wave to travel from source end to load end and vice-versa. At  t=400 [latex]\mu[/latex]s,  the voltage at the load end is
found to be 40 Volts. <br/><br/> The load resistance is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">25 Ohms</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">50 Ohms</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">75 Ohms</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">100 Ohms</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/a84.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/a84.jpg"/></noscript><br/>10V reflects to add to 30V in phase and gives 40V<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \Gamma &amp;=\frac{V_{r}}{V_{i}}=\frac{10}{30}=\frac{1}{3} \\ \Gamma &amp;=\frac{Z_{L}-Z_{0}}{Z_{L}+Z_{0}} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \frac{1}{3} &amp;=\frac{Z_{L}-50}{Z_{L}+50} \\ \Rightarrow \quad Z_{L} &amp;=100 \Omega \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 154
% Number: Question 154
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A mast antenna consisting of a 50 meter long vertical conductor operates over a
perfectly conducting ground plane. It is base-fed at a frequency of 600 kHz. The
radiation resistance of the antenna is Ohms is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2\pi ^{2}}{5}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\pi ^{2}}{5}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4\pi ^{2}}{5}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]20 \pi ^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
A mast antenna is a tower antenna or monopole  vertically erected on the ground for low frequency  ground wave communication.<br/>[latex] l=50 \mathrm{m}, \quad \lambda=500[/latex]<br/>[latex] l=\frac{\lambda}{10} [/latex]electrically short monopole<br/>[latex] R_{r}=20 \pi^{2}\left(\frac{d l}{\lambda}\right)^{2} \times 2[/latex]<br/> This 2 in the multiplication is due to image in conducting earth.<br/>[latex] R_{r}=\frac{2 \pi^{2}}{5} \simeq 4 \Omega[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 155
% Number: Question 155
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A rectangular wave guide having [latex]TE_{10}[/latex] mode as dominant mode is having a cut off frequency 18-GHz for the mode [latex]TE_{30}[/latex]. The inner broad-wall dimension of the rectangular wave guide is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{3}cm[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5 cm</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{2}cm[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">10 cm</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \mathrm{TE}_{30^{\prime}} \quad \lambda_{c}&amp;=\frac{2 a}{m}=\frac{2 a}{3} \\ \lambda_{c} &amp;=\frac{c}{t}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{18 \times 10^{9}}=\frac{1}{60} \\ \frac{1}{60} &amp;=\frac{2 a}{3} \\ \Rightarrow \quad a &amp;=\frac{1}{40}=2.5 \mathrm{cm}=\frac{5}{2} \mathrm{cm} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 156
% Number: Question 156
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">When a planes wave traveling in free-space is incident normally on a medium
having [latex]\varepsilon_{r}=4.0[/latex], the fraction of power transmitted into the medium is given by</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{8}{9}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{3}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{6}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} P_{t}&amp;=\left(1-\Gamma^{2}\right) P_{t} \\ \Gamma&amp;=\frac{\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}}{\eta_{2}+\eta_{1}}=\frac{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0} \epsilon_{r}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}}}{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0} \epsilon_{r}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}}} \\ \Gamma&amp;=\frac{\frac{1}{2}-1}{\frac{1}{2}+1}=\frac{-1}{3} \\ \tau&amp;=1-\Gamma^{2}=1-\frac{1}{9}=\frac{8}{9} \\ \therefore \quad \quad P_{t}&amp;=\tau \cdot P_{i} \\ \frac{P_{t}}{P_{i}}&amp;=\frac{8}{9} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 157
% Number: Question 157
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A medium is divide into regions I and II about x = 0 plane, as shown in the
figure below. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q67.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q67.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>An electromagnetic wave with electric field [latex]E_{1}=4\hat{a}_{x}+3\hat{a}_{y}+5\hat{a}_{z}[/latex] is incident normally on the interface from region I . The electric file [latex]E_2[/latex] in region II at the interface is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]E_{2}=E_{1}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]4\hat{a}_{x}+0.75\hat{a}_{y}-1.25\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]3\hat{a}_{x}+3\hat{a}_{y}+5\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-3\hat{a}_{x}+3\hat{a}_{y}+5\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \vec{E}_{t_{1}} &amp;=3 \hat{a}_{y}+5 \hat{a}_{z}=\vec{E}_{t_{2}} \\ \vec{D}_{n_{1}} &amp;=\vec{D}_{n_{2}} \\ \epsilon_{2} \vec{E}_{n_{2}} &amp;=\epsilon_{1} \vec{E}_{n_{1}} \\ \vec{E}_{n_{2}} &amp;=\frac{3 \times 4 \hat{a}_{x}}{4}=3 \hat{a}_{x} \\ \therefore \quad \vec{E}_{2} &amp;=\vec{E}_{t_{2}}+\vec{E}_{n_{2}} \\ &amp;=3 \hat{a}_{y}+5 \hat{a}_{z}+3 \hat{a}_{x} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 162
% Number: Question 162
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Voltage standing wave pattern in a lossless transmission line with characteristic
impedance 50[latex]\Omega[/latex] and a resistive load is shown in figure. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q87.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q87.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> The value of the load resistance is</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} S &amp;=\frac{V_{\max }}{V_{\min }}=\frac{4}{1}=4 \\ S &amp;=\frac{1+\Gamma}{1-\Gamma} \\ Z_{\max } &amp;=Z_{0} \cdot S \\ Z_{\min } &amp;=Z_{0} / S \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> The digram shows that minima is at load<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad Z_{L}=Z_{\min }=\frac{50}{4}=12.5 \Omega[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 163
% Number: Question 163

% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Two identical and parallel dipole antennas are kept apart by a distance of [latex]\frac{\lambda }{4}[/latex] in the H-plane. They are fed with equal currents but the right most antenna has a phase shift of +[latex]90^{\circ}[/latex]. The radiation pattern is given as.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q77.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q77.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
H-plane means [latex]\phi[/latex] pattern. <br/> Both dipoles are horizontally isotropic in the  H-plane.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} E &amp;=2 E_{0} \cos (\psi / 2) \\ \psi &amp;=\alpha+\beta d \cos \theta \\ &amp;=\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \cdot \frac{\lambda}{4} \cos \theta \\ \psi &amp;=\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{\pi}{2} \cos \theta \\ \psi=0 &amp; \; \cos \theta=-1 &amp; \theta_{\max }=180^{\circ} \\ \psi=\pi &amp;\; \cos \theta=1 &amp; \theta_{N P}=0^{\circ} \\ \psi=\frac{\pi}{2}&amp; \; \cos \theta=0 &amp;\theta_{\text{HPP}}=90^{\circ} / 270^{\circ} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Option (C) when [latex]\theta[/latex] is considered as shown in array calculations <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/a77.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/a77.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>

% QUESTION 166
% Number: Question 166
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Refractive index of glass is 1.5. Find the wavelength of a beam of light with
frequency of [latex]10^{14}[/latex] Hz in glass. Assume velocity of light is 3 \times  [latex]10^{8}[/latex] m/s in vacuum</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3 [latex]\mu[/latex]m</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3 mm</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2 [latex]\mu[/latex]m</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1 [latex]\mu[/latex]m</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\text { Refractive index }=\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}}[/latex]<br/> For glass with respective air [latex]=\sqrt{\epsilon_{R}} [/latex]<br/>[latex] V_{p}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{R}} \times f}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{1.5 \times 10^{14}}=2 \mu \mathrm{m}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The magnetic field intensity vector of a plane wave is given by <br/>[latex]\bar{H}(x,y,z,t)=10sin(50000t+0.004x+30)\hat{a}_{y} [/latex]<br/> where [latex]\hat{a}_{y}[/latex], denotes the unit vector in y direction. The wave is propagating with a phase velocity.</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]5 \times 10^{4}m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-3 \times 10^{8}m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]-1.25 \times 10^{7}m/s[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3 \times 10^{8}m/s[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \omega&amp;=50,000 ; \beta=0.004 \\ v_{p}&amp;=\frac{\omega}{\beta}=\frac{5 \times 10^{4}}{4 \times 10^{-3}}=1.25 \times 10^{7} \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 169
% Number: Question 169
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A lossless transmission line is terminated in a load which reflects a part of the incident power. The measured VSWR is 2. The percentage of the power that is reflected back is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">57.73</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">33.33</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.11</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">11.11</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\mathrm{SWR}=\frac{1+\Gamma}{1-\Gamma}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \quad 2=\frac{1+\Gamma}{1-\Gamma} [/latex]<br/> Voltage reflection coefficient<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \quad \Gamma=\frac{1}{3} [/latex]<br/> Power reflection coefficient<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad \frac{P_{\text {ref }}}{P_{\text {inc }}}=\Gamma^{2}=\frac{1}{9} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow 11.11 \%[/latex] of [latex]P_{i}[/latex] is reflected</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 170
% Number: Question 170
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A plane electromagnetic wave propagating in free space is incident normally on a large slab of loss-less, non-magnetic, dielectric material with [latex]\varepsilon &gt;\varepsilon _{0}[/latex]. Maxima and minima are observed when the electric field is measured in front of the slab. The maximum electric field is found to be 5 times the minimum field. The intrinsic impedance of the medium should be</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]120 \pi \Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]60 \pi \Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]600 \pi \Omega[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]24 \pi \Omega[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text{SWR} &amp;=\frac{E_{\max }}{E_{\min }} \\ \Rightarrow \quad s &amp;=\frac{5 E_{\min }}{E_{\min }}=5 \\ &amp; 5=\frac{1+|\Gamma|}{1-|\Gamma|}\;|\Gamma|=\frac{2}{3} \\ &amp; \eta_{2}=\frac{120 \pi}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}} \text { and } \eta_{2}=120 \pi \\ \text { As } &amp; \eta_{2} \lt \eta_{1} \\ \Rightarrow -\frac{2}{3}&amp;=\frac{\eta_{2}-120 \pi}{\eta+120 \pi} \\ \Rightarrow \eta_{2}&amp;=24 \pi \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 172
% Number: Question 172
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If [latex]\vec{E}=(\hat{a}_{x}+j\hat{a}_{y})e^{jkz-k\omega t} [/latex] and [latex]\vec{H}=(k/\omega \mu )(\hat{a}_{y}+j\hat{a}_{x})e^{jkz-j\omega t}[/latex], the time-averaged Poynting vector is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">null vector</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](k/\omega \mu )\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](2k/\omega \mu )\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](k/2\omega \mu )\hat{a}_{z}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \vec{P}_{\mathrm{avg}}=&amp; \frac{1}{2} \text{Re}\left[\vec{E} \times \vec{H}^{*}\right] \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{2} \text{Re}\left[\left(\hat{a}_{x}+j \hat{a}_{y}\right) e^{j k z-j \omega t}\right.\\ &amp;\left.\times\frac{k}{ \omega\mu}\left(\hat{a}_{x}+j \hat{a}_{y} \right)e^{j k z-j \omega t} \right ]\\ \vec{P}_{\mathrm{avg}}=&amp; \frac{k}{2 \mu \omega}[0]=0 \\ \left[\begin{array}{cc}1 &amp;j\\-j&amp;1\end{array}\right]=&amp;1+j^{2}=1-1=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

The magnitude of the voltage at the open circuit end of the line is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20041/q85.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20041/q85.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">

% QUESTION 175
% Number: Question 175
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A parallel plate air-filled capacitor has plate area of [latex]10^{-4} m^{2}[/latex] and plate
separation of [latex]10^{-3}[/latex] m. It is connected to a 0.5V, 3.6 GHz source. The

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.59 mA</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Displacement current,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} I_{d} &amp;=A J_{d} \\ &amp;=A \frac{\partial D}{\partial t}=A \in \frac{\partial E}{\partial t} \\ \left|I_{d}\right| &amp;=|A \in \omega E|=A \omega \in \frac{V}{d} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> After putting values, we get,<br/>[latex] I_{d}=10 \mathrm{mA}[/latex]</div>

efficiency is 100%. In such case whole of the
power fed to antenna is radiated in the form of
radiation. Therefore, if power fed to antenna is
1 mW then total radiated power is also 1 mW.</div>

% QUESTION 178
% Number: Question 178
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Two identical antennas are placed in the [latex]\theta =\pi /2[/latex] plane as shown in Fig. The elements have equal amplitude excitation with [latex]180^{\circ}[/latex] polarity difference, operating at wavelength [latex]\lambda[/latex]. The correct value of the magnitude of the far-zone resultant electric field strength normalized with that of a single element, both computed for [latex]\phi[/latex] = 0, is  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q90.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q90.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2\cos (\frac{2\pi s}{\lambda })[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2\sin (\frac{2\pi s}{\lambda })[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2\cos (\frac{\pi s}{\lambda })[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]2\sin (\frac{\pi s}{\lambda })[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]0 = 90^{\circ}[/latex] means XY plane means Z = O plane<br/> Far zone calculations of field at [latex]\phi[/latex] = O means point<br/> on X-axis.<br/> Angle made by the array axis to the point of<br/> consideration, if taken as [latex]\theta[/latex].<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a90.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a90.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text { Normalized array factor }&amp;=2\left|\cos \frac{\psi}{2}\right| \\ \psi&amp;=\beta d \cos \theta+\alpha \\ d&amp;=\sqrt{2} s, \theta=45^{\circ}, \alpha=180^{\circ} \\ \Rightarrow 2\left|\cos \frac{\psi}{2}\right| &amp;=2\cos\left[\frac{\beta d \cos \theta+\alpha}{2} \right ]\\ =&amp; 2 \cos \left[\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda .2} \sqrt{2} \operatorname{scos} 45^{\circ}+\frac{180^{\circ}}{2}\right] \\ =&amp; 2 \cos \left[\frac{\pi s}{\lambda}+90^{\circ}\right]\\ = &amp; 2 \sin \left(\frac{\pi \mathrm{s}}{\lambda}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 179
% Number: Question 179
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A rectangular metal wave guide filled with a dielectric material of relative permittivity [latex]\varepsilon _{r}[/latex] = 4 has the inside dimensions 3.0 cm [latex]\times[/latex] 1.2 cm. The cut-off frequency for the dominant mode is</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">12.5 GHz</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} v &amp;=\frac{c}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}}=\frac{3 \times 10^{8}}{2}=1.5 \times 10^{8} \\ f_{c} &amp;=\frac{v}{\lambda_{c}}=\frac{1.5 \times 10^{8}}{6 \times 10^{-2}}=2.5 \mathrm{GHz} \\ \text { or, } f_{c} &amp;=\frac{v}{2 a}=\frac{1.5 \times 10^{8}}{6 \times 10^{-2}}=2.5 \mathrm{GHz} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 180
% Number: Question 180
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Transmission Lines
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A short - circuited stub is shunt connected to a transmission line as shown in fig. If [latex]Z_{0}[/latex] = 50 ohm, the admittance Y seen at the junction of the stub and the transmission line is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q88.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q88.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">(0.01 - j0.02) mho</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(0.02 - j0.01) mho</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(0.04 - j0.02) mho</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(0.02 + j0) mho</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a88.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a88.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \beta &amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \\ \text { For } Y_{d} &amp; \beta d=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \times \frac{\lambda}{2}=\pi \\ &amp; Z_{d}=\frac{Z_{0}\left[Z_{L}+j Z_{0} \tan \beta d\right.}{Z_{0}+j Z_{L} \tan \beta d} \\ &amp; Z_{d}=\frac{50[100+j 50 \tan \pi]}{(50+j 100 \tan \pi)}=100 \\ Y_{d}&amp;=\frac{1}{Z_{d}}=\frac{1}{100}=0.01\\ For Y_{s} \quad \beta d&amp;=\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} \times \frac{\lambda}{8}=\frac{\pi}{4}, Z_{L} \rightarrow 0 \\ Z_{s}&amp;=\frac{Z_{0}\left[Z_{L}+j Z_{0} \tan \frac{\pi}{4}\right]}{\left[Z_{0}+j Z_{L} \tan \frac{\pi}{4}\right]}=j Z_{0} \\ Y_{s}&amp;=\frac{1}{Z_{s}}=\frac{1}{j Z_{0}}=-0.02 j\\ \therefore\quad Y&amp;=Y_{d}+Y_{s}=0.01-0.02 j\end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 181
% Number: Question 181

% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If the electric field intensity associated with a uniform plane electromagnetic wave traveling in a perfect dielectric medium is given by<br/> [latex]E(z,t)=10\cos (2\pi 10^{7}t-0.1\pi z)[/latex] V/m, then the velocity of the traveling wave is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3.00\times 10^{8}[/latex] m/sec</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]2.00\times 10^{8}[/latex] m/sec</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]6.28\times 10^{7}[/latex] m/sec</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2.00\times 10^{7}[/latex] m/sec</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \omega=2 \pi \times 10^{7} \\ \beta=0.1 \pi \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Comparing with [latex]A \cos (\omega t-\beta z) [/latex]<br/>[latex] v=\frac{\omega}{\beta}=\frac{2 \pi \times 10^{7}}{0.1 \pi}=2 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{m}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 182
% Number: Question 182
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A uniform plane wave traveling in air is incident on the plane boundary between air and another dielectric medium with [latex]\varepsilon _{r}[/latex] = 4. The reflection coefficient for the normal incidence, is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">zero</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.5\angle 180^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.333\angle 0^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.333\angle 180^{\circ}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \Gamma &amp;=\frac{\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}}{\eta_{z}+\eta_{1}} \\ &amp;=\frac{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0} \epsilon_{r}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{E_{0}}}}{\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0} \epsilon_{r}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\epsilon_{0}}}}=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}}-1}{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{r}}}+1},\epsilon_{r}=4\\ &amp;=\frac{-1 / 2}{3 / 2}=\frac{-1}{3} \Rightarrow 0.333 \angle 180^{\circ} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 183
% Number: Question 183
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If the electric field intensity is given by [latex]E = (xu_{x}+yu_{y}+zu_{z})[/latex] volt/m, the potential difference between X(2,0,0) and Y(1,2,3) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]+1[/latex] volt</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-1[/latex] volt</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]+5[/latex] volt</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]+6[/latex] volt</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} V &amp;=-\int \vec{E} \cdot \vec{d} \mid \\ &amp;=-\left[\int_{1}^{2} x d x \vec{u}_{x}+\int_{2}^{0} y d y \vec{u}_{y}+\int_{3}^{0} z d z \vec{u}_{z}\right] \\ &amp;=-\left[\left.\frac{x^{2}}{2}\right|_{1} ^{2}+\left.\frac{y^{2}}{2}\right|_{2} ^{0}+\left.\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right|_{3}\right] \\ &amp;=-\frac{1}{2}\left[2^{2}-1^{2}+0^{2}-2^{2}+0^{2}-3^{2}\right] \\ &amp;=-\frac{1}{2} x-10=5 \mathrm{V} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 184
% Number: Question 184
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Medium 1 has the electrical permittivity [latex]\varepsilon _{1}=1.5\varepsilon _{0}[/latex] farad/m and occupies the region to the left of x = 0 plane. Medium 2 has the electrical permittivity [latex]\varepsilon _{2}=2.5\varepsilon _{0}[/latex] farad/m and occupies the region to the right of x = 0 plane. If [latex]E_{1}[/latex] in medium 1 is [latex]E_{1}[/latex] = [latex](2u_{x}-3u_{y}+1u_{z})[/latex] volt/m, then  [latex]E_{2}[/latex] in medium 2 is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](2.0u_{x}-7.5u_{y}+2.5u_{z})[/latex] volt/m</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](2.0u_{x}-2.0u_{y}+0.6u_{z})[/latex] volt/m</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex](1.2u_{x}-3.0u_{y}+1.0u_{z})[/latex] volt/m</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](1.2u_{x}-2.0u_{y}+0.6u_{z})[/latex] volt/m</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \vec{E}_{1} &amp;=2 u_{x}-3 u_{y}+1 u_{z} \\ \vec{E}_{1 t} &amp;=-3 u_{y}+u_{z}=E_{2 t} &amp;(x=0 \text { plane}) \\ \vec{E}_{1 n} &amp;=2 u_{x} \\ \vec{D}_{1 n} &amp;=\vec{D}_{2 n}=\epsilon \vec{E} \\ \Rightarrow \epsilon_{1} \vec{E}_{1 n} &amp;=\epsilon_{2} \vec{E}_{2 n} \\ &amp;=1.5 \epsilon_{0} \cdot 2 u_{x}=2.5 \epsilon_{0} \cdot \vec{E}_{2 n} \\ \vec{E}_{2 n} &amp;=\frac{3}{2.5} u_{x}=1.2 u_{x} \\ \vec{E}_{2} &amp;=\vec{E}_{2 t}+\vec{E}_{2 n} \\ \vec{E}_{2} &amp;=-3 u_{y}+u_{z}+1.2 u_{x} \\ &amp;=1.2u_{x}-3.0u_{y}+1.0u_{z} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 185
% Number: Question 185
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The depth of penetration of electromagnetic wave in a medium having conductivity [latex]\sigma[/latex] at a frequency of 1 MHz is 25 cm. The depth of penetration at a frequency of 4 MHz will be</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">6.25 dm</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">12.50 cm</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">50.00 cm</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">100.00 cm</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \text { For conductors, } \delta=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\omega \mu \sigma}} \\ \qquad \begin{aligned} \delta \propto &amp; \frac{1}{\sqrt{f}} \\ \frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}=&amp; \sqrt{\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}} \\ \delta_{2}=&amp; \sqrt{\frac{1 \mathrm{MHz}}{4 \mathrm{MHz}}} \times 25 \mathrm{cm}=12.5 \mathrm{cm} \end{aligned} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 186
% Number: Question 186
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The unit of [latex]\bigtriangledown \times H[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Ampere</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Ampere/meter</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]Ampere/meter^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Ampere-meter</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \nabla \times \vec{H} &amp;=\frac{\partial \bar{D}}{\partial t}+\vec{J} \\ \frac{1}{m} \times \frac{\text { Ampere }}{m} &amp;=\frac{A}{m^{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 187
% Number: Question 187
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A person with a receiver is 5 Km away from the transmitter. What is the distance
that this person must move further to detect a 3-dB decrease in signal strength?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">942m</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2070m</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">4978m</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5320m</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \text { Signal strength }=\frac{P}{4 \pi R^{2}} \\ P_{1}=\frac{P}{4 \pi(5000)^{2}} \text { and } P_{2}=\frac{P}{4 \pi(x)^{2}} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Given, [latex]\quad \frac{P_{2}}{P_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}[/latex] (3 dB decrease)<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \Rightarrow\left(\frac{5000}{x}\right)^{2}&amp;=\frac{1}{2} \\ \Rightarrow \quad x&amp;=5000 \sqrt{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex]Required distance<br/>[latex] =5000 \sqrt{2}-5000=2071 \mathrm{m}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 188
% Number: Question 188
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Basics of Electromagnetics
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The electric field on the surface of a perfect conductor is 2 V/m. The conductor is
immersed in water with [latex]\epsilon =80\epsilon_{0}[/latex]. The surface charge density on the conductor is ([latex]\epsilon =10^{-9}/(36\pi)F/m[/latex] )</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0 \; C/m^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2 \; C/m^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.8 \times 10^{-11} \; C/m^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.14 \times 10^{-9} \; C/m^{2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The surface change density is equally to normal<br/> flux density <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} D_{\text {normal }} &amp;=\rho_{s} \\ D &amp;=\rho_{s}=\epsilon E=80 \cdot \epsilon \cdot 2 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 189
% Number: Question 189
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Uniform Plane Waves
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Distilled water at 25[latex]^{\circ}[/latex] C is characterized by [latex]\sigma[/latex] = 1.7 \times [latex]10^{-4}[/latex] mho/m and [latex]\epsilon =78\epsilon_{0}[/latex] at a frequency of 3 GHz. Its loss tangent [latex]tan\delta[/latex]
is (  [latex]\epsilon=10^{-9} /(36\pi) [/latex] F/m)</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.3 \times 10^{-5}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.3 \times 10^{-3}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.7 \times 10^{-4}[/latex]/78</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1.7 \times 10^{-4}/(78\epsilon_{0})[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Loss} \text { tangent } &amp;=\frac{\sigma}{\omega \in} \\ &amp;=\frac{1.7 \times 10^{-4}}{2 \pi \times 3 \times 10^{9} \times 78 \epsilon_{0}} \\ &amp;=\frac{1.7 \times 10^{-4} \times 9 \times 10^{9}}{3 \times 10^{9} \times 39} \\ &amp;=0.13 \times 10^{-4}=1.3 \times 10^{-5} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 191
% Number: Question 191
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Waveguides
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The phase velocity for the TE10-mode in an air-filled rectangular waveguide is (c
is the velocity of plane waves in free space)</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">less than c</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">equal to c</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">greater than c</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">none of the above</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} V_{p}&amp;=\frac{c}{\cos \theta}=\frac{c}{\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{f_{c}}{f}\right)^{2}}} \\ \text { with } f&amp;&gt;f_{c^{\prime}} V_{p}&gt;c \text { always. } \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 194
% Number: Question 194
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Antennas
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The line-of-sight communication requires the transmit and receive antenna to
face each other. If the transmit antenna is vertically polarized, for best reception
the receive antenna should be</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">horizontally polarized</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">vertically polarized</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">at [latex]45^{\circ}[/latex] with respect to horizontal polarization</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">at [latex]45^{\circ}[/latex] with respect to vertical polarization</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Both transmitting and receiving antennas should
be oriented correctly for maximum induction in the
receiver.
Transmitting antenna is vertical produced E field
is vertical inducing Efield is vertical so receiving
antenna has to be vertical.</div>
